{"parentUuid":"f73048dd-6e5d-4b05-b4d6-386ac06c70b6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MWZ5pgRVT36gJWdG6vHkT8","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# CLAUDE.md\n2\t\n3\t## What This Is\n4\t\n5\tTrading Desk — an active commodity book and portfolio management toolkit. Generates interactive HTML dashboards from JSON configs for tracking positions, escalation triggers, and trade execution.\n6\t\n7\t## Quick Start\n8\t\n9\t```bash\n10\t# Generate HTML dashboard from config\n11\tpython3 tools/commodity-book/bookgen.py tools/commodity-book/iran-hormuz-2026.json -o book.html\n12\t\n13\t# With live Yahoo Finance prices\n14\tpython3 tools/commodity-book/bookgen.py tools/commodity-book/iran-hormuz-2026.json -o book.html --fetch\n15\t\n16\t# Full pipeline (generate + validate + screenshot + publish to Reading Room)\n17\tpython3 tools/commodity-book/bookgen.py tools/commodity-book/iran-hormuz-2026.json \\\n18\t  -o book.html --fetch --validate --screenshot --publish --username amo\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\t## Architecture\n22\t\n23\t### Commodity Book Generator (`tools/commodity-book/bookgen.py`)\n24\t\n25\tTransforms a declarative JSON config into a complete interactive HTML trading dashboard.\n26\t\n27\t**JSON config → bookgen.py → single-file HTML dashboard**\n28\t\n29\tThe generated HTML has 4 tabs:\n30\t1. **Dashboard** — stats bar (book value, P&L, return, SGOV available), action summary, market data inputs, execution rules\n31\t2. **Book** — instrument cards with position entry, P&L, range bars (stop→current→target), SGOV deployment ledger, overlay categories\n32\t3. **Triggers** — 9-type escalation trigger state machine with close-date logging, binary toggles, constraint indicators\n33\t4. **Journal** — typed entries (trade/review/trigger/note/setup), filter chips, auto-logging, export/import JSON\n34\t\n35\t### JSON Config Schema\n36\t\n37\tSee `bookgen.py walkthrough` for full schema docs. Key sections:\n38\t- `instruments[]` — core portfolio (ticker, monthly allocation, targets, stops)\n39\t- `triggers[]` — escalation triggers (numeric thresholds, binary toggles, constraints)\n40\t- `overlays[]` — conditional instruments unlocked by triggers\n41\t- `marketFields[]` — editable market data inputs\n42\t- `fetchSymbols` — Yahoo Finance symbol mappings for live price fetch\n43\t- `rules[]` — execution rules (HTML strings)\n44\t- `situationUpdate` — current intelligence briefing\n45\t- `provenance` — sources, methodology, limitations\n46\t\n47\t### Live Price Fetch\n48\t\n49\tUses allorigins.win CORS proxy → Yahoo Finance v7 spark API (no API key needed). Fetches ETF/stock prices, commodity futures (BZ=F, CL=F, GC=F), and calculates curve spreads from Brent deferred months.\n50\t\n51\t### Integration with Reading Room\n52\t\n53\tThe `--publish` flag uploads the generated HTML as an article to the Reading Room platform. Requires `--username` and `RR_PASSWORD` env var.\n54\t\n55\t## Current Books\n56\t\n57\t| Config | Thesis | Monthly |\n58\t|---|---|---|\n59\t| `iran-hormuz-2026.json` | Iran/Hormuz crisis — overweight producers, fertilizer, gold; services capped | $8,000/mo |\n60\t\n61\t## Project Conventions\n62\t\n63\t- One JSON config per thesis/book\n64\t- bookgen.py is the single generator — all HTML dashboards are generated, not hand-built\n65\t- Screenshots use the infographic-gen skill's `screenshot.py` script\n66\t"}]},"uuid":"955694fc-a719-474a-8175-8c681f67c083","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:37:29.172Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f73048dd-6e5d-4b05-b4d6-386ac06c70b6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

{"parentUuid":"ef06fcbd-f2af-4677-8676-de1efc5ee942","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01M6csQ3kpZNNLBpHwYLuJGG","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# tradingDesk — Project Plan\n2\t\n3\t## What This Is\n4\t\n5\tA thesis-driven judgment toolkit for commodity and macro trading. Not an execution engine, not a portfolio tracker, not a trading bot. A system that organizes thesis-driven positions, tracks escalation triggers, integrates prediction market probabilities, and enforces discipline through structured decision frameworks.\n6\t\n7\tThe commodity book HTML is the seed. Everything grows from that pattern: JSON config → Python generator → self-contained interactive HTML dashboard.\n8\t\n9\t## Core Thesis\n10\t\n11\tThe market doesn't reward information. It rewards judgment — knowing what to do with information before everyone else figures it out. tradingDesk exists to sharpen that judgment by:\n12\t\n13\t1. Forcing structured thinking (triggers, not narratives)\n14\t2. Integrating signals from different domains (commodity prices, prediction markets, macro data, positioning)\n15\t3. Making the decision framework auditable (journal, trigger logs, snapshot history)\n16\t4. Challenging assumptions (constraint triggers that BLOCK action, not just trigger it)\n17\t\n18\t---\n19\t\n20\t## Architecture\n21\t\n22\t```\n23\t                    ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n24\t                    │         JSON Book Configs            │\n25\t                    │  (iran-hormuz.json, china-trade.json)│\n26\t                    └──────────────┬──────────────────────┘\n27\t                                   │\n28\t                    ┌──────────────▼──────────────────────┐\n29\t                    │        Market Snapshot Engine         │\n30\t                    │  tools/data-fetch/fetch.py            │\n31\t                    │                                       │\n32\t                    │  Yahoo Finance ─┐                     │\n33\t                    │  Polymarket ────┤                     │\n34\t                    │  Kalshi ────────┤→ snapshots/         │\n35\t                    │  FRED ──────────┤   YYYY-MM-DD.json   │\n36\t                    │  EIA ───────────┤                     │\n37\t                    │  CFTC COT ─────┘                     │\n38\t                    └──────────────┬──────────────────────┘\n39\t                                   │\n40\t                    ┌──────────────▼──────────────────────┐\n41\t                    │        Signal Evaluation Engine       │\n42\t                    │  tools/signals/check.py               │\n43\t                    │                                       │\n44\t                    │  Loads config + snapshot               │\n45\t                    │  Evaluates all triggers                │\n46\t                    │  Detects state changes                 │\n47\t                    │  Emits alerts (email/Telegram/webhook) │\n48\t                    │  Archives signal report                │\n49\t                    └──────────────┬──────────────────────┘\n50\t                                   │\n51\t          ┌────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┐\n52\t          │                        │                        │\n53\t          ▼                        ▼                        ▼\n54\t┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────┐\n55\t│  Commodity Book  │  │ Polymarket       │  │ Master Dashboard     │\n56\t│  (per thesis)    │  │ Tracker          │  │ (multi-book view)    │\n57\t│  bookgen.py      │  │ polytracker.py   │  │ mastergen.py         │\n58\t│                  │  │                  │  │                      │\n59\t│  4 tabs:         │  │  Event tracking  │  │  Cross-book exposure │\n60\t│  Dashboard       │  │  Probability     │  │  Sector concentration│\n61\t│  Book            │  │  timeseries      │  │  Aggregate P&L       │\n62\t│  Triggers        │  │  Crude oil       │  │  Shared SGOV ledger  │\n63\t│  Journal         │  │  synthetic chain │  │  Conflict detection  │\n64\t└─────────────────┘  │  Iran/geopolitics│  └──────────────────────┘\n65\t                     │  Cross-ref with  │\n66\t                     │  trigger states  │\n67\t                     └──────────────────┘\n68\t```\n69\t\n70\tAll outputs are **self-contained HTML files**. No web server. No database. State in localStorage + exportable JSON. This is intentional — the HTML file is both the tool and the artifact.\n71\t\n72\t---\n73\t\n74\t## Phased Build Plan\n75\t\n76\t### Phase 1: Foundation (immediate)\n77\t\n78\t**Goal:** Clean up bookgen.py for extensibility, add Polymarket tracker, add multi-source data fetch.\n79\t\n80\t**1a. Extract JS/CSS from bookgen.py**\n81\tThe #1 maintainability problem: ~40KB of minified JS and ~700 lines of CSS are inlined as Python string constants. Extract to:\n82\t```\n83\ttools/commodity-book/\n84\t  bookgen.py          # generator (reads template files)\n85\t  template.js         # extracted JS logic (~500 lines, formatted)\n86\t  template.css        # extracted CSS (~700 lines, formatted)\n87\t  iran-hormuz-2026.json\n88\t```\n89\tbookgen.py reads and injects them at generation time. Same output, but now the JS/CSS can be edited with proper tooling.\n90\t\n91\t**1b. Polymarket Tracker** (`tools/polymarket/`)\n92\tNew generator: `polytracker.py` — takes a JSON config of tracked events/markets and generates a self-contained HTML dashboard.\n93\t\n94\tConfig example:\n95\t```json\n96\t{\n97\t  \"title\": \"Polymarket — Iran/Oil/Trump\",\n98\t  \"watchlist\": [\n99\t    {\"slug\": \"will-us-forces-enter-iran-by-april-30\", \"alias\": \"US in Iran (Apr 30)\"},\n100\t    {\"slug\": \"crude-oil-cl-hit-120-high-by-end-of-march\", \"alias\": \"CL $120 Mar\"}\n101\t  ],\n102\t  \"events\": [\n103\t    {\"slug\": \"crude-oil-prices-march\", \"alias\": \"Crude Oil Strikes (29 markets)\"}\n104\t  ],\n105\t  \"refreshInterval\": 300\n106\t}\n107\t```\n108\t\n109\tDashboard features:\n110\t- Event cards with current probability, 24h/1wk change, volume\n111\t- Price history sparklines (CLOB API `/prices-history`)\n112\t- The crude oil 29-market event rendered as a **synthetic options chain** — strike prices on Y axis, probabilities on X axis\n113\t- Cross-reference panel linking Polymarket odds to commodity book triggers (e.g., \"Polymarket says 4.7% chance CL hits $120 this month — your Brent persistence trigger needs $115 for 3 closes\")\n114\t- Auto-refresh via allorigins.win CORS proxy to Gamma API (no auth needed)\n115\t\n116\t**Also track Kalshi** (server-side fetch during generation):\n117\t- CPI monthly predictions (KXCPI)\n118\t- Fed funds rate after FOMC (KXFED)\n119\t- GDP growth predictions (KXGDP)\n120\tThese are the most valuable prediction market signals for macro positioning.\n121\t\n122\t**1c. Multi-source data fetcher** (`tools/data-fetch/fetch.py`)\n123\tSingle script that pulls from all sources and writes a timestamped snapshot:\n124\t\n125\t```bash\n126\tpython3 tools/data-fetch/fetch.py -o snapshots/2026-03-30.json\n127\t```\n128\t\n129\tSources (Phase 1):\n130\t| Source | Data | Auth | CORS |\n131\t|---|---|---|---|\n132\t| Yahoo Finance v7/v8 | Prices, futures, curves | None | Proxy |\n133\t| Polymarket Gamma | Geopolitical probabilities | None | Proxy |\n134\t| Kalshi | CPI/Fed/GDP predictions | None | Server only |\n135\t| FRED | Macro series (rates, breakevens, credit spreads) | Free key | Proxy |\n136\t| EIA v2 | Diesel, crude inventory | Free key | Direct |\n137\t| CFTC COT (Socrata) | Speculative positioning | None | Direct |\n138\t| BLS | Employment, CPI | None | Direct |\n139\t\n140\tOutput: `snapshots/YYYY-MM-DD.json` with all data normalized into a flat structure that bookgen.py and polytracker.py can consume.\n141\t\n142\t**1d. Wire snapshot into bookgen.py**\n143\tNew flag: `bookgen.py config.json --snapshot snapshots/2026-03-30.json -o book.html`\n144\tMerges snapshot data into market fields and instrument prices before generating.\n145\t\n146\t---\n147\t\n148\t### Phase 2: Signal Engine\n149\t\n150\t**Goal:** Headless trigger evaluation with push notifications.\n151\t\n152\t`tools/signals/check.py` — loads a book config + latest snapshot, evaluates all triggers, compares against previous state, emits alerts on changes.\n153\t\n154\t```bash\n155\t# Manual check\n156\tpython3 tools/signals/check.py tools/commodity-book/iran-hormuz-2026.json \\\n157\t  --snapshot snapshots/2026-03-30.json\n158\t\n159\t# Output:\n160\t# APPROACHING: Brent Persistence — $112.57 / $115 (97.9%)\n161\t# APPROACHING: Fertilizer Stress — $683 / $700 (97.6%)\n162\t# BLOCKED: Rig Confirmation — rigs still falling (543)\n163\t# STABLE: 6 other triggers unchanged\n164\t\n165\t# With notifications\n166\tpython3 tools/signals/check.py config.json --snapshot latest \\\n167\t  --notify telegram --notify email\n168\t```\n169\t\n170\tRun manually 3x/week (Mon/Wed/Fri per execution rules) or put on a cron.\n171\t\n172\tFuture: composite triggers (AND/OR logic), relative triggers (rate of change), time-windowed conditions.\n173\t\n174\t---\n175\t\n176\t### Phase 3: Multi-Book Aggregation\n177\t\n178\t**Goal:** Run multiple thesis books simultaneously with cross-book risk awareness.\n179\t\n180\tNew configs:\n181\t```\n182\tbooks/\n183\t  iran-hormuz-2026.json      # existing\n184\t  agriculture-shock-2026.json # fertilizer cascade thesis\n185\t  master.json                 # references both, defines cross-book rules\n186\t```\n187\t\n188\tMaster dashboard (generated by `mastergen.py` or `bookgen.py --multi`):\n189\t- Tab per book (renders same 4-tab layout within each)\n190\t- Aggregate tab: total exposure by sector, shared instrument overlap, net SGOV deployment, conflict detection (book A says buy CF, book B says sell CF)\n191\t- Single unified journal across all books\n192\t\n193\t---\n194\t\n195\t### Phase 4: Enhanced Journal\n196\t\n197\t**Goal:** Turn the journal from a trade log into a decision audit trail.\n198\t\n199\tEach entry captures:\n200\t1. What triggered the action (trigger ID, market data snapshot)\n201\t2. What the action was (deploy, hold, trim, close)\n202\t3. What the alternative was (what you chose NOT to do)\n203\t4. Outcome (filled in later: P&L, whether trigger thesis played out)\n204\t\n205\tAnalytics:\n206\t- Win rate by trigger type\n207\t- Average R-multiple by setup type\n208\t- Trigger accuracy (how often a fired trigger led to a profitable action)\n209\t- \"Edge Finder\" — cross-reference all journal metadata to surface patterns\n210\t\n211\tJournal data stored as append-only JSONL (one file per book). HTML dashboard renders it. The JSONL file is the durable record.\n212\t\n213\t---\n214\t\n215\t### Phase 5: Historical Analysis\n216\t\n217\t**Goal:** Backtest trigger accuracy against archived snapshots.\n218\t\n219\tWith snapshots archived daily:\n220\t- \"Would trigger X have fired on date Y?\"\n221\t- \"What was the P&L outcome 30/60/90 days after trigger Z fired?\"\n222\t- Trigger threshold calibration: \"If I set Brent persistence at $110 instead of $115, how many more signals would have fired?\"\n223\t- Overlay trigger accuracy chart in the dashboard\n224\t\n225\t---\n226\t\n227\t## Data Source {"parentUuid":"84c29ea6-51bb-4574-b0dc-e2c8066316f6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0145EHxuHat22P2zJ5UjgDyL","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"NetworkX weighted directed graph node attributes Python DAG causal modeling\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"networkx.algorithms.dag — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/_modules/networkx/algorithms/dag.html\"},{\"title\":\"causalnex.structure.StructureModel — causalnex 0.12.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://causalnex.readthedocs.io/en/stable/causalnex.structure.StructureModel.html\"},{\"title\":\"Tutorial — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/tutorial.html\"},{\"title\":\"n2_2_weighted_and_directed_graphs\",\"url\":\"https://transport-systems.imperial.ac.uk/tf/60008_21/n2_2_weighted_and_directed_graphs.html\"},{\"title\":\"Weighted Graph — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/auto_examples/drawing/plot_weighted_graph.html\"},{\"title\":\"DiGraph—Directed graphs with self loops — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/reference/classes/digraph.html\"},{\"title\":\"Building DAGs / Directed Acyclic Graphs with Python - MungingData\",\"url\":\"https://www.mungingdata.com/python/dag-directed-acyclic-graph-networkx/\"},{\"title\":\"Directed Acyclic Graphs — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/reference/algorithms/dag.html\"},{\"title\":\"Graph types — NetworkX 3.7rc0.dev0 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/latest/reference/classes/index.html\"},{\"title\":\"NetworkX Tutorial — algorithmx 1.1.2 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://algorithmx-python.readthedocs.io/en/latest/examples/networkx-tutorial.html\"}]\n\nThe search has returned results on using NetworkX for weighted directed graphs, DAGs, and node/edge attributes in Python. Here are the key findings:\n\n## NetworkX Weighted Directed Graphs and Node Attributes\n\n**Graph Structure:**\nAttributes such as weights, labels, colors, or whatever Python object you like, can be attached to graphs, nodes, or edges, with each graph, node, and edge holding key/value attribute pairs in an associated attribute dictionary.\n\n**Creating Weighted Directed Graphs:**\nDiGraph is short for \"directed graph\" and the directed graph is modeled as a list of tuples that connect the nodes. You can use `DG.add_weighted_edges_from([(1, 2, 0.5), (3, 1, 0.75)])` to add weighted edges to a DiGraph.\n\n**Node and Edge Attributes:**\nTo change edge data attributes in a DiGraph, use two sets of brackets: `G.edges[1, 2]['weight'] = 4`. Weighted degree using edge data attributes is provided via `V = G.degree(weight='attr_name')` where any string with the attribute name can be used.\n\n**DAGs and Causal Modeling:**\nNetworkX provides algorithms for directed acyclic graphs (DAGs), though most of these functions are only guaranteed to work for DAGs. CausalNex can add weighted causal relationships between nodes with relationships given origin labels like \"unknown\", \"learned\" or \"expert\".\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"b9ea926f-d698-43df-8607-83c6930a9134","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:37:51.222Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"84c29ea6-51bb-4574-b0dc-e2c8066316f6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

There are 2 versions on NPM, but only the one in the DagreJs org is receiving updates right now. Dagre is licensed under the terms of the MIT License.\n\n### Key Features\n\nIt provides completely client-side computed layout, and Dagre requires only very basic information to lay out graphs, such as the dimensions of nodes. You're free to render the graph using whatever technology you prefer.\n\n## Dagre-D3\n\nThe dagre-d3 library acts as a front-end to dagre, providing actual rendering using D3. This makes it useful for visualizing the graph layouts that Dagre computes.\n\n## Algorithm Details\n\nThe general skeleton for Dagre comes from Gansner, et al., \"A Technique for Drawing Directed Graphs\", which gives both an excellent high level overview of the phases involved in layered drawing. Besides the basic skeleton, the technique described in the paper produces an acyclic graph, and uses the network simplex algorithm for ranking.\n\n## Installation\n\nYou can install Dagre from npm using `npm i dagre` or `npm i @dagrejs/dagre` for the actively maintained version.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"1468c8ee-604a-43da-8f45-c402382e0e8a","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:37:53.840Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c058bfa8-20a1-4b26-8b10-d41d7dc1f369","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

Here's what the search results reveal about Cytoscape.js vs Vis.js vs D3 for DAG graph visualization in 2025-2026:\n\n## Key Comparison\n\n### Learning Curve & Ease of Use\nCytoscape has a steeper learning curve due to its extensive features and capabilities, with developers needing time to familiarize themselves with its API and functionalities, especially for advanced graph analysis. In contrast, vis-network is designed for quick implementation and has a gentle learning curve. D3 has a steep learning curve that requires a pretty steep learning curve, as the library is very powerful but you'd have to invest a decent amount of time in wrapping your head around its concepts.\n\n### Performance\nVis is the slowest of the bunch being an order of magnitude slower than the competitors, and when optimized for large graphs it performed somewhat better, but not much when compared to the other two. Cytoscape excels in handling complex networks with thousands of nodes and edges, providing advanced algorithms for layout and analysis, allowing users to visualize intricate relationships effectively.\n\n### Use Case Recommendations\nChoose Cytoscape if you need a robust library for complex graph analysis and visualization, especially for biological data or large networks, as it offers extensive layout algorithms and supports interactive features. Opt for vis-network if you need a straightforward solution for network visualization with an emphasis on ease of use and interactivity. D3.js is best for complete creative control and custom-built visualizations, giving you complete control over every pixel, making it the top choice for creating unique, artistic, or highly custom data visualizations.\n\n### 2025-2026 Ecosystem Overview\nIn 2025, D3.js is a highly flexible data visualization library providing granular control over visuals and interactions for custom relationship maps; Cytoscape.js is a graph/network-focused library optimized for performance and rich interactivity, ideal for large and dynamic graphs; and Vis.js offers user-friendly network visualizations with built-in physics simulation for natural layouts and quick prototyping.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3c251e56-72c6-4958-924f-6e51a2a87297","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:37:55.520Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"49ed65ac-7577-4ffe-96b7-df3fadcd54f0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

pgmpy/pgmpy: Python library for Causal AI · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy\"},{\"title\":\"Bayesian Network — 1.0.0 | pgmpy docs\",\"url\":\"https://pgmpy.org/detailed_notebooks/2.%20Bayesian%20Networks.html\"},{\"title\":\"[2304.08639] pgmpy: A Python Toolkit for Bayesian Networks\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.08639\"},{\"title\":\"Learning Bayesian Networks from Data — 1.0.0 | pgmpy docs\",\"url\":\"https://pgmpy.org/detailed_notebooks/10.%20Learning%20Bayesian%20Networks%20from%20Data.html\"},{\"title\":\"Dynamic Bayesian Network (DBN) — 1.0.0 | pgmpy docs\",\"url\":\"https://pgmpy.org/models/dbn.html\"},{\"title\":\"pgmpy: A Python Toolkit for Bayesian Networks\",\"url\":\"https://www.jmlr.org/papers/volume25/23-0487/23-0487.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"A GUIDE TO BAYESIAN NETWORKS SOFTWARE PACKAGES FOR\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.17025\"},{\"title\":\"(PDF) pgmpy: A Python Toolkit for Bayesian Networks\",\"url\":\"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/370103424_pgmpy_A_Python_Toolkit_for_Bayesian_Networks\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about pgmpy and the concepts mentioned in your query:\n\n## pgmpy Overview\n\npgmpy is a Python package for causal inference and probabilistic inference using Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs) and Bayesian Networks with a focus on modularity and extensibility.\n\n## Key Features\n\nIt implements algorithms for structure learning, parameter estimation, approximate and exact inference, causal inference, and simulations. The library implements several inference algorithms like variable elimination, belief propagation, etc.\n\n## Inference Algorithms\n\nRegarding propagation algorithms specifically, Belief Propagation (BP) is an algorithm that splits the query computation into two parts – model calibration and query computation. It can be much faster than VE when multiple queries need to be made on the same model.\n\n## Design Philosophy\n\npgmpy is written in pure python for readability with a focus on code modularity. This makes it lightweight and accessible for Python users working with Bayesian networks.\n\n## DAG Support\n\nThe library has comprehensive support for DAGs as part of its Bayesian Network models, allowing users to represent network structures with conditional probability distributions (CPDs).\n\nThe search results don't contain specific information about propagation thresholds, but the library's modular design allows for customization of inference algorithms to meet different computational requirements.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"352db55f-766f-4a76-bf96-48638703ee9b","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:04.921Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f9f923fd-ed20-4570-aba0-0d6a8278fab6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}
{"parentUuid":"856d581c-5a2a-424d-8e53-fbb337ad74ff","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WX6Bt9LpDMNRkFRF6feXpQ","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Monte Carlo simulation DAG propagation probability graph scenario analysis\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"What is The Monte Carlo Simulation? - The Monte Carlo Simulation Explained - AWS\",\"url\":\"https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/monte-carlo-simulation/\"},{\"title\":\"Monte Carlo method - Wikipedia\",\"url\":\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo_method\"},{\"title\":\"What Is Monte Carlo Simulation? | IBM\",\"url\":\"https://www.ibm.com/think/topics/monte-carlo-simulation\"},{\"title\":\"Chapter 2 Simulation of Random Variables and Monte Carlo | Lecture notes for “Introduction to Stochastic Processes”\",\"url\":\"https://gordanz.github.io/stochastic-book/simulation-of-random-variables-and-monte-carlo.html\"},{\"title\":\"VOSE | How Does Monte Carlo Simulation Work?\",\"url\":\"https://www.vosesoftware.com/Articles/Monte-Carlo-simulation-explained.php\"},{\"title\":\"Monte Carlo Simulation - an overview | ScienceDirect Topics\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/computer-science/monte-carlo-simulation\"},{\"title\":\"Monte Carlo Simulation | Statgraphics\",\"url\":\"https://www.statgraphics.com/monte-carlo\"},{\"title\":\"Monte Carlo Simulation software: Risk analysis and assessment\",\"url\":\"https://www.palisade.com/risk/monte_carlo_simulation.asp\"},{\"title\":\"INTRODUCTION TO MONTE CARLO SIMULATION Samik Raychaudhuri\",\"url\":\"https://www.informs-sim.org/wsc08papers/012.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Monte Carlo Analysis: A Powerful Tool for Risk Management · Riskonnect\",\"url\":\"https://riskonnect.com/reporting-analytics/monte-carlo-analysis-a-powerful-tool-for-risk-management/\"}]\n\nI've completed a web search for \"Monte Carlo simulation DAG propagation probability graph scenario analysis.\" Here are the key findings:\n\n## Monte Carlo Simulation Overview\n\nMonte Carlo methods are a broad class of computational algorithms based on repeated random sampling, with the underlying concept being to use randomness to solve deterministic problems. A Monte Carlo Simulation builds a model of possible results by leveraging a probability distribution for any variable that has inherent uncertainty.\n\n## Core Methodology\n\nA typical Monte Carlo simulation includes one or more input variables X (some of which follow a probability distribution), one or more output variables Y whose distribution is desired, and a mathematical model coupling the X's and the Y's. Monte Carlo simulations sample from a probability distribution for each variable to produce hundreds or thousands of possible outcomes, and the results are analyzed to get probabilities of different outcomes occurring.\n\n## Scenario Analysis\n\nScenario analysis determines which input variables contribute significantly toward reaching a particular goal (called the target scenario or output scenario) associated with an output. Monte Carlo analysis in risk management involves using a GRC platform to run thousands of simulations to generate a range of possible outcomes for a particular risk scenario or project.\n\n## Advantages in Decision-Making\n\nMonte Carlo Simulations have assessed the impact of risk in many real-life scenarios and provide advantages over predictive models with fixed inputs, such as the ability to conduct sensitivity analysis or calculate the correlation of inputs.\n\n**Note:** The search results did not contain specific information about DAG (Directed Acyclic Graph) propagation in the context of Monte Carlo simulations. Here are the key findings:\n\n## DoWhy Overview\n\nDoWhy is a Python library for causal inference that supports explicit modeling and testing of causal assumptions, based on a unified language for causal inference combining causal graphical models and potential outcomes frameworks.\n\n## Graph Propagation and Scenario Analysis\n\nDoWhy's features for graphical causal models allow users to automatically pinpoint the main drivers of particular outcomes with only a few lines of code. DoWhy features include arrow strengths, intrinsic causal influences, anomaly attribution and distribution change attribution.\n\nDoWhy's graphical causal model framework is based on Pearl's graphical causal model framework and models the causal data generation process of each variable explicitly via causal mechanisms.\n\n## Core Workflow\n\nDoWhy presents an API for the four steps common to any causal analysis: 1) modeling the data using a causal graph and structural assumptions, 2) identifying whether the desired effect is estimable under the causal model, 3) estimating the effect using statistical estimators, and 4) refuting the obtained estimate through robustness checks and sensitivity analyses.\n\n## Practical Applications\n\nA practical example involves analyzing product profit that depends on factors like sold units and operational costs, where sold units depend on page visitors, price, and promotions.\n\nThe search results provide official documentation, tutorials, and practical examples of using DoWhy for causal inference tasks in Python.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"89f80766-189b-413c-84aa-0a750f527e27","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:08.238Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d956996f-eca3-4d15-b4c9-66f3fae09821","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

The Tigramite documentation is at · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/jakobrunge/tigramite\"},{\"title\":\"Published as a conference paper at ICLR 2023 CAUSAL REPRESENTATION LEARNING FOR\",\"url\":\"https://openreview.net/pdf?id=itZ6ggvMnzS\"},{\"title\":\"Causal Discovery from Temporal Data: An Overview and New Perspectives | ACM Computing Surveys\",\"url\":\"https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3705297\"},{\"title\":\"Causal Discovery with Multivariate Time Series Data | by Kenneth Styppa | Causality in Data Science | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/causality-in-data-science/causal-discovery-with-multivariate-time-series-data-a3f7ffc16747\"},{\"title\":\"Temporal latent variable structural causal model for causal discovery under external interferences - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231225009531\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about time-lagged causal graphs, temporal DAG modeling, and shock propagation with delay edges:\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n**Temporal DAG Structure with Time Lags:**\nFull-time causal graphs have vertices consisting of components at each time point with lag-specific directed links such as x_i^(t-k) → x_j^t, enabling representation of delayed causal effects across time periods.\n\n**Window Causal Graphs:**\nWindow causal graphs are proposed by assuming a time-homogeneous causal structure, which simplifies discovery by assuming the causal relationships repeat across time slices.\n\n## Shock Propagation Modeling\n\nLagged interactions between nodes are fundamental for modeling shock propagation since they allow to flexibly describe these lagged interactions between nodes. Leveraging on vector autoregressive (VAR) models, an Impulse Response Function (IRF) modification for temporal networks has been proposed to analyze how shocks propagate through network structures.\n\n## Causal Discovery Algorithms\n\nThe PCMCI algorithm leverages a variant of the PC algorithm that flexibly combines linear or nonlinear conditional independence tests and extracts causal relations from time-series data, with the goal of discovering the window causal graph.\n\nStructural vector autoregression (SVAR) describes linear dependencies between nodes that are either instantaneous, if the nodes are at the same time point, or lagged if there is a time delay between the nodes.\n\n## Practical Applications\n\nThe approach infers the temporal causal graph structure among monitoring variables or sensors using time-lagged and contemporaneous causal discovery algorithms, which is particularly useful for root cause analysis in complex systems.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"2c4cd810-f767-4ed6-9135-8948748d18da","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:21.335Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6057fe8b-9b2f-4bb7-ba41-5855fbbbca4e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

| Analytica\",\"url\":\"https://analytica.com/decision-technologies/influence-diagrams/\"},{\"title\":\"Influence Diagram | Influence Diagram Template | Creately\",\"url\":\"https://creately.com/usage/influence-diagram/\"},{\"title\":\"Influence Diagram - What is an Influence Diagram\",\"url\":\"https://www.smartdraw.com/influence-diagram/\"},{\"title\":\"Influence Diagrams – BayesFusion\",\"url\":\"https://www.bayesfusion.com/influence-diagrams/\"},{\"title\":\"Influence Diagram | Cross-Functional Flowchart | Influence Diagram Software | Draw Decision Tree Online\",\"url\":\"https://www.conceptdraw.com/examples/draw-decision-tree-online\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about influence diagram decision graph scenario planning software tools:\n\n## What is an Influence Diagram?\n\nAn influence diagram is an intuitive visual display of a decision problem that depicts the key elements, including decisions, uncertainties, and objectives as nodes of various shapes and colors, with influences among them shown as arrows. It is typically made up of 3 types of nodes (decisions, uncertainties, and values) and influencing arcs.\n\n## Key Software Tools\n\nSeveral tools are available for creating influence diagrams and decision analysis:\n\n1. **DPL (Decision Programming Language)** - DPL offers an easy-to-use decision tree modeling environment that provides analysts with a comprehensive set of robust decision framing and analysis tools, which combined with reliable Excel spreadsheet linking support can help provide insights necessary to make the best, most informed strategic decisions.\n\n2. **Analytica** - Analytica extends the standard influence diagram notation with additional types of node, including module nodes that allow you to organize a complex model as a hierarchy of modules, enabling you to organize a model containing hundreds, or even thousands, of variables into a hierarchy of diagrams.\n\n3. **ConceptDraw** - ConceptDraw DIAGRAM is a powerful drawing and vector diagram maker software that allows to design professional looking Influence Diagrams quick and easy.\n\n4. **Other Tools** - Additional options include Miro, Creately, EdrawMax, and SmartDraw, which provide collaborative features and templates for creating influence diagrams.\n\n## Applications\n\nIn a project management context, influence diagrams are important for mitigating risk and predicting uncertainty that could lead to poor project outcomes, and when strategic planning, influence diagrams can help you choose the best course of action for the future of your business.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"52656675-771b-489e-8a59-8c09db6b0280","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:22.249Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"51992173-13dc-4630-89f9-5a0385cb69f6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

Jackson ‡\",\"url\":\"https://www.pietrotebaldi.com/uploads/8/1/3/6/81364040/centralityaxioms_feb_13_2023.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Graph Centrality Measures: Types and Explanation.\",\"url\":\"https://www.turing.com/kb/graph-centrality-measures\"},{\"title\":\"Unveiling Influence in Networks: A Novel Centrality Metric and Comparative Analysis through Graph-Based Models | MDPI\",\"url\":\"https://www.mdpi.com/1099-4300/26/6/486\"}]\n\nI've performed a web search for your query about graph convergence, fan-in analysis, signal confluence, scoring, and node centrality. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Overview of Node Centrality Measures\n\nCentrality measures the relative importance of a node within a graph. Various measures such as degree centrality, closeness centrality, betweenness centrality, eigenvector centrality, and Katz centrality are commonly used to analyze social influence within networks.\n\n## Graph Convergence and Centrality\n\nFor large enough parameters, diffusion centrality converges to eigenvector centrality if the discount factor is larger than the inverse of the largest eigenvalue of the adjacency matrix, and exactly at the inverse of the largest eigenvalue, Katz-Bonacich and eigenvector centrality converge.\n\n## Signal Processing and Node Scoring\n\nThe Graph Laplacian matrix and its eigenvalues are important for systems modelled by a graph, as they can explain the graph's connectivity and support Graph Signal Processing. Node centrality metrics measure the relevance of a node in a graph according to topological criteria.\n\n## Composite Scoring Approaches\n\nRecent research has developed more sophisticated node scoring methods. The CDP score is a composite measure that accentuates degree centrality while incorporating the number of paths, and uses squared degrees while considering the number of simple paths.\n\nThe search results primarily focus on established centrality metrics (degree, betweenness, closeness, eigenvector) and their mathematical properties rather than \"fan-in analysis\" or \"signal confluence\" specifically, which appear to be more specialized or domain-specific terms not extensively covered in these academic sources.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"878348b4-2c08-48fa-aefa-05b08ab92f02","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:33.131Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b4f50061-7b5b-4abf-ac8d-7f5483058401","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

Here are the key findings related to causal graphs, macro trading, geopolitical transmission, and chain analysis in financial contexts:\n\n## Key Findings:\n\n**Transmission Channels & Causal Frameworks:**\nTwo primary transmission channels have been identified: the financial channel, where uncertainty and heightened risk aversion drive shifts in investment portfolios and capital flows, and the real economy channel, which impacts global trade, supply chains, and commodities.\n\n**Causal Analysis Methods:**\nResearch focuses on tracking how macro, policy, and geopolitical shocks transmit through markets using daily regime detection, risk mapping, causal transmission, and cross-asset monitoring. Network-theoretic tools are applied to the Granger causal graph linking macroeconomic and policy-related indicators, using centrality measures, path lengths, and node-specific scores such as betweenness and bridging coefficients to infer transmission pathways through which uncertainty diffuses.\n\n**Multi-Step Causal Chains:**\nSeveral multi-step causal chains emerge, highlighting the hierarchical and sectoral propagation of uncertainty. For example, monetary policy shocks can manifest in downstream healthcare uncertainty via regulatory channels, and fiscal instability can transmit into consumer price uncertainty through tax policy.\n\n**Risk Propagation in Trade Networks:**\nRisk transmission mechanisms and domino effects within trade and financial networks show how corporate failures can propagate along supply chains and between interconnected sectors, generating systemic impacts at different levels of the global economy.\n\n**Geopolitical-Market Linkages:**\nGeopolitical uncertainty significantly impacts investor sentiment, market stability, and price expectations, with American markets demonstrating faster adjustment to geopolitical uncertainty compared to their Asian counterparts.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"c15d04a4-afc6-4dbd-882d-f4d2f0060ce5","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:34.286Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6d5b3d81-29ce-4deb-aa25-3d7bfe4cc09d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

mermaid-js/mermaid: Generation of diagrams like flowcharts or sequence diagrams from text in a similar manner as markdown · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mermaid-js/mermaid\"},{\"title\":\"How to Use the Mermaid JavaScript Library to Create Flowcharts\",\"url\":\"https://www.freecodecamp.org/news/use-mermaid-javascript-library-to-create-flowcharts/\"},{\"title\":\"Usage | Mermaid\",\"url\":\"https://mermaid.js.org/config/usage.html\"},{\"title\":\"Building Interactive Flowcharts with Mermaid.js and JavaScript | by Hari Babu Dornala | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://haridornala.medium.com/building-interactive-flowcharts-with-mermaid-js-and-javascript-57ec27cdc63d\"},{\"title\":\"Mermaid | Diagramming and charting tool\",\"url\":\"https://mermaid.js.org/\"},{\"title\":\"Online FlowChart & Diagrams Editor - Mermaid Live Editor\",\"url\":\"https://mermaid.live/\"},{\"title\":\"Mastering Mermaid.js: The Complete Guide to Creating Stunning Diagrams with Code | Antoine Griffard\",\"url\":\"https://antoinegriffard.com/posts/mermaid-js-comprehensive-guide/\"},{\"title\":\"Online Mermaid Visual Editor | Mermaid Flow\",\"url\":\"https://www.mermaidflow.app/\"},{\"title\":\"Flowchart Diagrams | mermaid-js/mermaid | DeepWiki\",\"url\":\"https://deepwiki.com/mermaid-js/mermaid/3.1-flowchart-diagrams\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for your Mermaid.js query covering flowcharts, DAG rendering, CDN usage, file size, and interactive features:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Rendering & DAG Layout:**\nThe default renderer is dagre, and starting with Mermaid version 9.4, you can use an alternate renderer named elk, which is better for larger and/or more complex diagrams. Dagre is the default layout engine for flowcharts using a hierarchical ranking algorithm.\n\n**CDN Usage:**\nYou can import mermaid from a CDN using `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mermaid@11/dist/mermaid.esm.min.mjs`, and the simplest way to use Mermaid is through a CDN in your HTML.\n\n**Library Size:**\nMermaid offers a smaller version that's approximately half the size of the full library, called Mermaid Tiny, which doesn't support Mindmap Diagrams, Architecture Diagrams, KaTeX rendering, or lazy loading.\n\n**Interactive & Clickable Features:**\nYou can add simple interactivity to Mermaid flowcharts when you have rendered the diagram in HTML with an associated JavaScript file. Flowcharts support click events and links when securityLevel is set to loose, though sandboxed rendering may hinder interactive functionality like scripts, popups, and links.\n\n**General Capabilities:**\nMermaid is a JavaScript-based diagramming and charting tool that uses Markdown-inspired text definitions and a renderer to create and modify complex diagrams.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"970caeb4-659a-4c0d-b95b-61f7c2c56b71","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:35.743Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c5f4e9ec-bc45-4c2e-af11-762f6660c4dd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

{"parentUuid":"299c1597-6a88-493c-b316-0539ccb8b440","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01395JTN3bmdTfjMaCitsFj6","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"CausalNex McKinsey Python causal graph scenario what-if analysis\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"GitHub - mckinsey/causalnex: A Python library that helps data scientists to infer causation rather than observing correlation. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mckinsey/causalnex\"},{\"title\":\"causalnex/README.md at develop · mckinsey/causalnex\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mckinsey/causalnex/blob/develop/README.md\"},{\"title\":\"Meet CausalNex, our new open-source library for causal reasoning and “what if” analysis\",\"url\":\"https://www.mckinsey.com/about-us/new-at-mckinsey-blog/causalnex-our-new-open-source-library-leverages-cause-and-effect-relationships-in-data\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing CausalNex — Driving Models Which Respect Cause And Effect | by QuantumBlack, AI by McKinsey | QuantumBlack, AI by McKinsey | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/quantumblack/introducing-causalnex-driving-models-which-respect-cause-and-effect-a561545f0a5e\"},{\"title\":\"What’s new in CausalNex v0.10?. Paul Beaumont, Data Scientist, Hiep… | by QuantumBlack, AI by McKinsey | QuantumBlack, AI by McKinsey | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/quantumblack/whats-new-in-causalnex-v0-10-67b16005e596\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - p2t2/causalnex: A Python library that helps data scientists to infer causation rather than observing correlation.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/p2t2/causalnex\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - sailfish009/causalnex: A Python library that helps data scientists to infer causation rather than observing correlation.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/sailfish009/causalnex\"},{\"title\":\"causalnex · PyPI\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/causalnex/\"},{\"title\":\"Causalnex Overview, Examples, Pros and Cons in 2025\",\"url\":\"https://best-of-web.builder.io/library/mckinsey/causalnex\"},{\"title\":\"causalnex/CONTRIBUTING.md at develop · mckinsey/causalnex\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mckinsey/causalnex/blob/develop/CONTRIBUTING.md\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about CausalNex:\n\n## Overview\n\nCausalNex is built on collective experience to leverage Bayesian Networks to identify causal relationships in data so that right interventions can be developed from analytics. It's a Python library that helps data scientists infer causation rather than observing correlation.\n\n## Key Features\n\nThe library uses state-of-the-art structure learning methods to understand conditional dependencies between variables, allows domain knowledge to augment model relationships, builds predictive models based on structural relationships, fits probability distributions of Bayesian Networks, evaluates model quality with standard statistical checks, simplifies how causality is understood in Bayesian Networks through visualization, and analyzes the impact of interventions using Do-calculus.\n\n## What-If Analysis and Scenario Testing\n\nCausalNex provides a practical 'what if' library, deployed to test scenarios using Bayesian Networks. Using CausalNex, data scientists can ask \"what if this underlying variable was different?\", and observe the size of the impact, which can help identify the highest-value features that can then be used to effect change.\n\n## Why McKinsey Created It\n\nLeveraging Bayesian Networks for end-to-end causality and counterfactual analysis often requires the use of at least three separate open source libraries, each with its own interface, making the process complex and hindering the potential power of Bayesian Networks. CausalNex aims to simplify this end-to-end process for causality and counterfactual analysis.\n\nThe library is maintained by QuantumBlack, McKinsey's advanced analytics arm, and is available as an open-source project on GitHub and PyPI.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"368ca28e-bea1-47f2-961d-20d4a895abb0","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:47.687Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"299c1597-6a88-493c-b316-0539ccb8b440","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

- Quora\",\"url\":\"https://www.quora.com/What-free-software-can-do-graphs-like-Palantir-Gotham\"},{\"title\":\"DataWalk vs. Palantir Gotham: A Cost-Effective Alternative for Link Analysis\",\"url\":\"https://datawalk.com/datawalk-vs-palantir-link-analysis/\"},{\"title\":\"Best Palantir Gotham Alternatives & Competitors\",\"url\":\"https://sourceforge.net/software/product/Palantir-Gotham/alternatives\"},{\"title\":\"Ask HN: Is there an open source equivalent to Palantir? | Hacker News\",\"url\":\"https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=13488795\"},{\"title\":\"Palantir Gotham Alternatives\",\"url\":\"https://crozdesk.com/software/palantir-gotham/alternatives\"},{\"title\":\"Gotham | Palantir\",\"url\":\"https://www.palantir.com/platforms/gotham/\"},{\"title\":\"DataWalk : The Palantir Alternative Copyright © DataWalk 2021 ® ®\",\"url\":\"https://www.dlt.com/sites/default/files/resource-attachments/2022-05/DataWalk-The-Palantir-Alternative.pdf\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about Palantir Gotham, graph analysis for intelligence, and open-source alternatives:\n\n## Palantir Gotham Overview\n\nPalantir Gotham is the operating system for global decision-making widely used by governmental, law enforcement and military agencies for tasks like intelligence analysis, law enforcement investigations, and decision-making in national security and on the battlefield. It allows users to fuse massive, disparate datasets into a unified model and use tools like link charts, graph algorithms, and geospatial mapping to uncover hidden connections and patterns.\n\n## Key Capabilities\n\nPalantir applications are built on top of the ontology, with data sources mapped onto this terminology allowing for multiple sources to be integrated into a single conceptual model. Palantir offers a very comprehensive feature set, packaging the best of breed tooling from data warehouses, ETL, machine learning, data integration, ontology, business intelligence, application development and delivery, and security.\n\n## Alternatives to Gotham\n\nSeveral commercial alternatives exist, with **DataWalk** being the most prominently mentioned: DataWalk provides comparable analytical power to Palantir Gotham but with transparent pricing, much faster deployment times, and an intuitive interface that empowers analysts without requiring specialized programming skills.\n\n## Open Source Alternatives\n\nThe search results indicate limited mature open-source alternatives. Lumify is a web-based, open source (available on Github), Java-based option, though it is challenging to get running. With the current discussion in Europe around digital sovereignty in mind, some are exploring whether Palantir could be replaced with a stack of Open Source technology. However, fully-fledged open-source equivalents appear to be limited compared to Palantir's comprehensive commercial platform.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"62d8a77e-10e5-4f35-814d-9e0eafca62fc","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:49.042Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7392878b-b403-453f-9b5c-15d759c73874","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

statsmodels 0.15.0 (+942)\",\"url\":\"https://www.statsmodels.org/dev/vector_ar.html\"}]\n\nI've completed the web search for your query about VAR vector autoregression, impulse response, oil, employment, transmission lag (3 months and 6 months). Here's a summary of the key findings:\n\n## Overview of VAR and Impulse Response Analysis\n\nVector autoregression (VAR) is a statistical model used to capture the relationship between multiple quantities as they change over time. Each variable's equation includes the variable's lagged (past) values, the lagged values of the other variables in the model, and an error term.\n\n## Impulse Response Functions\n\nImpulse response analysis is an important step in econometric analyses employing vector autoregressive models, with the main purpose of describing the evolution of a model's variables in reaction to a shock in one or more variables. This feature allows tracing the transmission of a single shock within an otherwise noisy system of equations, making them very useful tools in the assessment of economic policies.\n\nAn impulse-response function describes the evolution of the variable of interest along a specified time horizon after a shock in a given moment, and basically allows you to determine if there is causal effect on different variables based on the confidence interval.\n\n## Application to Oil and Economic Variables\n\nOne research study investigated the transmission and feedback mechanism between international crude oil prices and China's refined oil prices using the Granger causality test, vector autoregression model, impulse response function and variance decomposition methods.\n\nAn oil price shock (as an example of a supply shock) is typically assumed to be independent from other economic shocks.\n\nThe search results provide general frameworks for VAR analysis and impulse responses but do not contain specific research on 3-month and 6-month transmission lags between oil prices and employment. For detailed findings on those specific lag periods, you may want to search for published economic research directly addressing oil price shocks and labor market impacts.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"cebb3898-0342-4451-857f-efc341316f45","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:38:52.283Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ca989bce-c272-4fbc-b3a3-63b13b277012","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

Document\",\"url\":\"https://pydocs.github.io/p/networkx/2.8.2/api/networkx.algorithms.dag.lexicographical_topological_sort.html\"}]\n\nI found relevant information about your search query. Here's what the results contain:\n\n## NetworkX Topological Sort for Cascading Propagation\n\nA topological sort is a nonunique permutation of the nodes of a directed graph such that an edge from u to v implies that u appears before v in the topological sort order. This ordering is valid only if the graph has no directed cycles.\n\n### Cascade Simulation\n\nThe search results include relevant work on cascade propagation models. One approach simulates the independent cascade (IC) model using different network implementations including igraph and NetworkX. The model is a stochastic process where the expected spread of a given seed set is calculated by averaging over Monte Carlo simulations.\n\n### Key NetworkX Functions\n\nNetworkX uses Kahn's algorithm to perform topological sorting. Additionally, a topological generation is a node collection in which ancestors of a node in each generation are guaranteed to be in a previous generation.\n\n### Application to Scheduling\n\nA canonical application of topological sorting is in scheduling a sequence of jobs or tasks based on their dependencies, with jobs represented as vertices and edges showing dependencies, and topological sort giving an order to perform the jobs.\n\nThe results show that NetworkX provides multiple functions for ordering nodes in DAGs, which can be useful for propagating values through a graph structure in the order determined by dependencies.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"41aa7d2a-6ff2-4e07-bdae-6af050d88a41","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:02.386Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ca3599dd-4577-4a01-b1d6-a653c271d886","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}
{"parentUuid":"e721012b-a4df-48d8-9957-be3a189d45da","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011UfaUFGmMembvEJqwDTXQY","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"cytoscape.js dagre layout extension CDN example hierarchical\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"GitHub - cytoscape/cytoscape.js-dagre: The Dagre layout for DAGs and trees for Cytoscape.js · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js-dagre\"},{\"title\":\"Using layouts · Cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://blog.js.cytoscape.org/2020/05/11/layouts/\"},{\"title\":\"Cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://js.cytoscape.org/\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-dagre CDN by jsDelivr - A CDN for npm and GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://www.jsdelivr.com/package/npm/cytoscape-dagre\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-dagre examples - CodeSandbox\",\"url\":\"https://codesandbox.io/examples/package/cytoscape-dagre\"},{\"title\":\"Cytoscape.js Layouts: Klay and Dagre\",\"url\":\"https://codepen.io/keckelt/pen/dBOMxG\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape.js-dagre/README.md at master · cytoscape/cytoscape.js-dagre\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js-dagre/blob/master/README.md\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - n313893254/cytoscape.js-dagre: The Dagre layout for DAGs and trees for Cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/n313893254/cytoscape.js-dagre\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-dagre - npm\",\"url\":\"https://www.npmjs.com/package/cytoscape-dagre\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-dagre.js demo\",\"url\":\"https://js.cytoscape.org/demos/dagre-layout/\"}]\n\nI found relevant information about the Cytoscape.js Dagre layout extension. Here's a summary of the search results:\n\n## Overview\n\nThe dagre layout is a traditional hierarchical layout that looks like how people typically draw binary trees on a whiteboard with a nice inverted V shape from one level to the next, and should be one of the first layouts to try if your graph is a DAG.\n\n## Installation & Import\n\nThe library can be imported using ES import, CommonJS require, or AMD. For ES import: `import cytoscape from 'cytoscape'; import dagre from 'cytoscape-dagre'; cytoscape.use( dagre );`\n\n## CDN Usage\n\nThere is a free, fast, and reliable CDN for cytoscape-dagre available at jsDelivr (version 2.5.0).\n\n## Basic Usage\n\nTo call the layout: `cy.layout({ name: 'dagre', ...`\n\n## Configuration Options\n\nKey options include: rankDir (direction), ranker ('network-simplex', 'tight-tree' or 'longest-path'), minLen (number of ranks between source and target), edgeWeight (function for edge weight), fit (whether to fit to viewport), padding, spacingFactor, animate, and animationDuration.\n\n## Examples\n\nThere's a CodePen example showing dagre layout usage with configuration like `const dagreLayout = { name: 'dagre', rankDir: 'LR' };`\n\nThe official GitHub repository at `github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js-dagre` and the jsDelivr CDN provide the best sources for implementation details and live demos.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"f4ce86a5-cf25-4361-bda2-8df6e833b0cd","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:03.351Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e721012b-a4df-48d8-9957-be3a189d45da","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

{"parentUuid":"34573d7e-8463-4023-a6ed-49b9756c10d3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VPaphX3DMxxRnhR1VKwi3H","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"\"Market Ontology\" causal graph geopolitical intelligence macro regime detection\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Market Ontology – Macro, Policy & Geopolitical Intelligence\",\"url\":\"https://marketontology.com/\"},{\"title\":\"A Framework for Market State Prediction with Ontological Asset Selection: A Multimodal Approach\",\"url\":\"https://www.mdpi.com/2076-3417/15/3/1034\"},{\"title\":\"FinCaKG-Onto: the financial expertise depiction via causality knowledge graph and domain ontology | Applied Intelligence | Springer Nature Link\",\"url\":\"https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10489-025-06247-1\"},{\"title\":\"Free Real-Time Market Intelligence Dashboard for Traders | World Monitor Finance\",\"url\":\"https://www.worldmonitor.app/blog/posts/real-time-market-intelligence-for-traders-and-analysts/\"},{\"title\":\"NEXUS Intelligence — Geopolitical-Market Signal Platform\",\"url\":\"https://nexushq.xyz\"},{\"title\":\"Real-Time Geopolitical Sentiment & Market Effects\",\"url\":\"https://permutable.ai/geopolitical-sentiment/\"},{\"title\":\"World Monitor Pro — Markets, Macro & Geopolitical Intelligence\",\"url\":\"https://www.worldmonitor.app/pro\"},{\"title\":\"Causal Regime Detection in Energy Markets With\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.04361\"},{\"title\":\"Macro-founded machine learning models for power market price trend detection - ScienceDirect\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S1544612325009195\"},{\"title\":\"Market Regime Detection Artificial Intelligence (AI) Global Market analysis and Long-term Forecasts, 2019-2024, 2024-2029F, 2034F\",\"url\":\"https://finance.yahoo.com/news/market-regime-detection-artificial-intelligence-162800526.html\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about Market Ontology and related concepts:\n\n## Market Ontology Platform\n\nMarket Ontology is a platform designed to track how macro, policy, and geopolitical shocks transmit through markets, offering daily regime detection, risk mapping, causal transmission, and cross-asset monitoring in one terminal.\n\nThe platform combines data from 40+ sources into daily regime detection, risk mapping, causal transmission analysis, and cross-asset monitoring. It includes 45+ analytical modules across macro and monetary policy, markets and flows, political and geopolitical intelligence, capital markets, scenario analytics, and extended domains including energy, defense, housing, supply chain, and more.\n\n## Key Features\n\nThe platform allows users to see macro, policy, and geopolitical shifts before they fully transmit through rates, credit, equities, FX, and commodities, get a daily operating picture without stitching together multiple terminals, and translate legislation, trade, sanctions, lobbying, and supply disruptions into business and market implications.\n\n## Related Intelligence Approaches\n\nThe search results also reveal other platforms using similar methodologies:\n\n- NEXUS Intelligence uses four primary layers: Geopolitical (conflicts, sanctions, diplomacy), Market (options flow, volatility, credit spreads), OSINT (flight tracking, shipping, social media), and Systemic Risk (regime detection, macro indicators).\n\n- Permutable's platform extracts cause-and-effect relationships between political events and asset movements, allowing institutions to model and anticipate the market impact of global developments.\n\nThese platforms represent a broader trend of integrating causal graph analysis, geopolitical intelligence, and machine learning to detect macro regimes and predict market impacts.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"6fb23742-68e7-492d-bcc5-354387172100","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:06.563Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"34573d7e-8463-4023-a6ed-49b9756c10d3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

{"parentUuid":"6de24ddc-6bb6-4d70-b976-8d279d33237b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014NYY4QpKTBu3gDc6rjp8gf","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"cytoscape.js tooltip popup on node hover click event handler example\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"node/edge tooltip with onclick (instead of mouseover)\",\"url\":\"https://groups.google.com/g/cytoscapeweb-discuss/c/ykQar7waCjY\"},{\"title\":\"Tooltip.js demo · Issue #12 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js-popper\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js-popper/issues/12\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-qtip - npm\",\"url\":\"https://www.npmjs.com/package/cytoscape-qtip\"},{\"title\":\"Cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://js.cytoscape.org/\"},{\"title\":\"Qtip on mouseover · Issue #1580 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/1580\"},{\"title\":\"Tooltip On Hovering Node • shinyCyJS\",\"url\":\"https://jhk0530.github.io/shinyCyJS/articles/Tooltip-On-Hovering-Node.html\"},{\"title\":\"Mouseover events fire on neighbouring edges covered by nodes when the edge is assigned with 'top' for 'z-compound-depth' style · Issue #2171 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/2171\"},{\"title\":\"Re: Event coordinates in Cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://groups.google.com/g/cytoscape-discuss/c/ZHlNSPTPZvA\"},{\"title\":\"Mouse hover event on nodes\",\"url\":\"https://groups.google.com/g/cytoscape-app-dev/c/hSkC4FSJaCM\"},{\"title\":\"cytoscape-popper/README.md\",\"url\":\"https://unpkg.com/browse/cytoscape-popper@1.0.4/README.md\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here are some key approaches and examples for implementing tooltip/popup functionality on node hover and click events in Cytoscape.js:\n\n## Event Handlers\n\nYou can listen to events on nodes using `cy.on('tap', 'node', function(evt){ var node = evt.target; ... })`. For hover interactions, you can use `mouseover` and `mouseout` events instead of `tap`.\n\n## Popular Libraries for Tooltips\n\nIt is recommended to use cytoscape-popper with tippy.js instead of cytoscape-qtip, as the qtip2 library that cytoscape-qtip wraps is no longer maintained, whereas cytoscape-popper has proper support for npm+webpack and npm+browserify.\n\n## Popper.js Extension Example\n\nHere's a basic popper example: create a popper on the first node with `cy.nodes()[0].popper({ content: () => { let div = document.createElement('div'); div.innerHTML = 'Popper content'; document.body.appendChild(div); return div; }, popper: {} })`.\n\n## Older qTip Approach\n\nUsing the events exposed by Cytoscape.js, it is not hard to create a tooltip on the click event of a node or edge. However, this older library is no longer actively maintained.\n\nFor modern implementations, **cytoscape-popper** with **Tippy.js** is the recommended approach as it has better ecosystem support and integration with modern build tools.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"2348876f-e4bc-4169-a4f1-d34d32d85f0e","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:16.723Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6de24ddc-6bb6-4d70-b976-8d279d33237b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

— NetworkX 2.0rc1.dev20170908001755 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://dschult-networkx.readthedocs.io/en/latest/_modules/networkx/algorithms/dag.html\"}]\n\nI've retrieved search results for NetworkX functionality related to ancestors, descendants, shortest paths, weighted graphs, and DAGs. Here's a summary of the key information:\n\n## Shortest Path Algorithms\n\nNetworkX uses Breadth-First Search for unweighted graphs and Dijkstra's Algorithm for weighted graphs by default. Other variants of the shortest path problem such as all pairs of shortest paths are also supported.\n\n## Weighted Path Computation\n\nIn weighted graphs, the shortest path is the path with minimum sum of weights associated to path edges. The `dijkstra_path()` function computes the shortest weighted path between two nodes in a graph.\n\n## DAG-Specific Functions\n\nFor directed acyclic graphs (DAGs):\n\n- The `ancestors()` function returns all nodes having a path to source in G\n- The `descendants()` function returns all nodes reachable from source in G\n\n## Path Finding Options\n\nWhen both source and target are specified, shortest path functions return a single list of nodes; when neither are specified, they return an iterator over source-target pairs.\n\nThese tools are useful for analyzing graph connectivity, finding optimal paths in weighted networks, and working with DAG structures in NetworkX.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"67adc13a-fbb6-4381-8f97-a1e79650d359","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:18.197Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"75e44216-3349-42ee-ac23-066548cff920","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

erikbrinkman/d3-dag: Layout algorithms for visualizing directed acyclic graphs · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/erikbrinkman/d3-dag\"},{\"title\":\"Sugiyama | d3-dag\",\"url\":\"https://erikbrinkman.github.io/d3-dag/interfaces/Sugiyama.html\"},{\"title\":\"D3-DAG: Sugiyama / Erik Brinkman | Observable\",\"url\":\"https://observablehq.com/@erikbrinkman/d3-dag-sugiyama\"},{\"title\":\"d3-dag\",\"url\":\"https://erikbrinkman.github.io/d3-dag/modules.html\"},{\"title\":\"d3-dag/src/sugiyama/index.ts at main · erikbrinkman/d3-dag\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/erikbrinkman/d3-dag/blob/main/src/sugiyama/index.ts\"},{\"title\":\"Dynamic node size · erikbrinkman/d3-dag · Discussion #118\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/erikbrinkman/d3-dag/discussions/118\"},{\"title\":\"Creating a DAG Layout View with D3 and React | by Julian Toro | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@fulit103/creating-a-dag-layout-view-with-d3-and-react-4a9dee3230a4\"},{\"title\":\"d3 DAG visualization\",\"url\":\"https://groups.google.com/g/d3-js/c/_jf_9z36hCo\"},{\"title\":\"d3-dag/README.md at main · erikbrinkman/d3-dag\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/erikbrinkman/d3-dag/blob/main/README.md\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - znicholasbrown/d3-dag: Layout algorithms for visualizing directed acyclic graphs\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/znicholasbrown/d3-dag\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about d3-dag, the Sugiyama layout algorithm, and related information:\n\n## d3-dag Library Overview\n\nD3-dag provides layout algorithms for visualizing directed acyclic graphs, with Sugiyama being a robust layered layout option. The sugiyama method works by assigning each node a layer, shuffling the layers to minimize edge crossings, and then aligning nodes within a layer to produce a pleasing DAG layout.\n\n## Algorithm Structure\n\nThe Sugiyama algorithm consists of three main steps: Layering (assigning each node a non-negative integer layer), Decross (reordering nodes in each layer to minimize crossings), and Coord (assigning x and y coordinates that respect the layer, layer ordering, and size).\n\n## Layout Sizing and Dimensions\n\nThe layout operators return the width and height of the final layout as a LayoutResult, take a NodeSize parameter specifying node dimensions (either a constant or callback), and accept a gap parameter for minimum spacing between nodes.\n\n## CDN Usage\n\nD3-dag can be loaded via CDN using a script tag like `<script src=\"https://unpkg.com/d3-dag@1.1.0\"></script>`.\n\n## Library Status\n\nThe project is currently in light maintenance mode with simple feature requests potentially implemented, but no expansion to new use cases. At the time of creation, this was one of the only libraries to support layered graph layouts in JavaScript.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"f0438dfb-d6cd-4e37-80fe-e38002230c8b","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:18.794Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bd81a4b9-710e-4af0-afed-b2998e1033b4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

pgmpy/pgmpy: Python library for Causal AI · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy\"},{\"title\":\"pgmpy.models.DynamicBayesianNetwork — 1.0.0 | pgmpy docs\",\"url\":\"https://pgmpy.org/_modules/pgmpy/models/DynamicBayesianNetwork.html\"},{\"title\":\"Dynamic Bayesian Networks | pgmpy/pgmpy | DeepWiki\",\"url\":\"https://deepwiki.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/3.1.3-dynamic-bayesian-networks\"},{\"title\":\"Introduction to Dynamic Bayesian networks | Bayes Server\",\"url\":\"https://www.bayesserver.com/docs/introduction/dynamic-bayesian-networks/\"},{\"title\":\"(PDF) pgmpy: A Python Toolkit for Bayesian Networks\",\"url\":\"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/370103424_pgmpy_A_Python_Toolkit_for_Bayesian_Networks\"},{\"title\":\"Bayesian Network — 1.0.0 | pgmpy docs\",\"url\":\"https://pgmpy.org/detailed_notebooks/2.%20Bayesian%20Networks.html\"},{\"title\":\"pgmpy: A Python Toolkit for Bayesian Networks\",\"url\":\"https://www.jmlr.org/papers/volume25/23-0487/23-0487.pdf\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about pgmpy's Dynamic Bayesian Network implementation:\n\n## Overview\n\npgmpy is a Python package for causal inference and probabilistic inference using Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs) and Bayesian Networks with a focus on modularity and extensibility. It provides a uniform API for building, learning, and analyzing models, such as Bayesian Networks, Dynamic Bayesian Networks, Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs), and Structural Equation Models (SEMs).\n\n## Dynamic Bayesian Networks\n\nDynamic Bayesian Networks (DBNs) extend standard Bayesian networks to model temporal processes by representing random variables as they evolve over discrete time steps. \n\nDynamic Bayesian Networks in pgmpy are implemented using a 2-Time-Slice Bayesian Network (2-TBN) structure. This means: The network is defined by specifying the structure for the first two time slices, this structure is then unrolled to represent additional time slices as needed, and each node is represented as a pair of (node_name, time_slice). Edges can exist within a time slice (intra-slice) or between adjacent time slices (inter-slice).\n\n## Learning from Temporal Data\n\nThe algorithm iterates over every 2 consecutive time slices in the data and updates the CPDs based on it. The column names must be of the form (variable, time_slice) and the time-slices must start from 0.\n\n## Causal Modeling\n\nImplementations of various algorithms for Causal Discovery, Parameter Estimation, Approximate (Sampling Based) and Exact inference, and Causal Inference are available.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"9e834776-9b1e-4063-9ada-4e2957158cd7","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:19.982Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8c8ba9bc-a115-495c-92be-fbfcaf0bac78","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}
