Styles — Cytoscape User Manual 3.10.4 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://manual.cytoscape.org/en/stable/Styles.html\"},{\"title\":\"change edges line color when the connected node clicked in cytoscape.js · Issue #1277 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/1277\"},{\"title\":\"RCyjs: interactive network visualization using cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://www.bioconductor.org/packages/devel/bioc/vignettes/RCyjs/inst/doc/RCyjs.html\"},{\"title\":\"Change node style when hovering over node · Issue #2555 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/2555\"},{\"title\":\"Node colors change depending on zoom level · Issue #2360 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/2360\"},{\"title\":\"2.5.0 Changing colors/shapes of nodes not working properly · Issue #1156 · cytoscape/cytoscape.js\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/cytoscape/cytoscape.js/issues/1156\"},{\"title\":\"Cytoscape.js: how to change the node and edges styles when selecting them?\",\"url\":\"https://groups.google.com/g/cytoscape-discuss/c/JFJgu7mQe40\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's information about cytoscape.js node color state change, animation, style updates, and programmatic changes:\n\n## Key Approaches for Node Color Updates\n\n**1. Using Classes for State-Based Styling**\n\nNodes have the color associated with their class, or the default color if they have no class defined. If you change the class of a node at runtime its color changes accordingly. You can add or remove classes using:\n```javascript\ncy.$('#nodeId').addClass('className');\ncy.$('#nodeId').removeClass('className');\n```\n\n**2. Dynamic Style Mapping**\n\nYou can animate the display by successively updating node parameters, with rules that map node color and node size to the current value of data attributes using mapData functions which calculate visual attributes from the current value of the specified data attributes.\n\n**3. Direct Style Updates Using Data**\n\nYou can define selectors like `node[background_color]` with styles that map to data attributes, such as `'background-color': 'data(background_color)'`.\n\n**4. Batch Updates for Performance**\n\nA batch should correspond to a single visual operation and usually should contain calls only to functions for modifying state like `eles.data()`, `eles.addClass()`, `eles.removeClass()`, etc.\n\n**5. Animation Support**\n\nCytoscape.js supports animation through functions like `ele.animation()`, `cy.animate()`, etc.\n\n## Common Patterns\n\nFor real-time updates, you would typically:\n- Update node data properties using `.data()`\n- Add/remove classes that trigger style changes via CSS selectors\n- Use event listeners to trigger style changes on user interaction (hover, click, etc.)\n\nThe search results show this is an active area with developers seeking solutions for dynamic styling, indicating it's a common use case in cytoscape.js applications.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"5dcb8483-3154-43c3-8078-fb43039602d1","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:34.123Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"421b22d4-9a24-441d-97c2-632faa4cc844","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

{"parentUuid":"9cb3c6ef-f98a-4103-a6d5-cbd31cfda8d4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ca74b85b-0bd6-4994-9fb8-368416dfd0b4","agentId":"a750b81b7f1ecd446","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Vg8pXq43Sg4KP9wahVBuim","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"betweenness centrality in-degree weighted DAG convergence node importance signal strength\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Betweenness centrality - Wikipedia\",\"url\":\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betweenness_centrality\"},{\"title\":\"Centrality - Wikipedia\",\"url\":\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrality\"},{\"title\":\"Betweenness Centrality - Neo4j Graph Data Science\",\"url\":\"https://neo4j.com/docs/graph-data-science/current/algorithms/betweenness-centrality/\"},{\"title\":\"Node Centrality in Weighted Networks | Tore Opsahl\",\"url\":\"https://toreopsahl.com/tnet/weighted-networks/node-centrality/\"},{\"title\":\"Node-weighted centrality: a new way of centrality hybridization | Computational Social Networks | Full Text\",\"url\":\"https://computationalsocialnetworks.springeropen.com/articles/10.1186/s40649-020-00081-w\"},{\"title\":\"Centrality Tutorial\",\"url\":\"https://networkit.github.io/dev-docs/notebooks/Centrality.html\"},{\"title\":\"Betweenness Centrality and Other Essential Centrality Measures in Network Analysis\",\"url\":\"https://memgraph.com/blog/betweenness-centrality-and-other-centrality-measures-network-analysis\"},{\"title\":\"betweenness_centrality — NetworkX 3.6.1 documentation\",\"url\":\"https://networkx.org/documentation/stable/reference/algorithms/generated/networkx.algorithms.centrality.betweenness_centrality.html\"},{\"title\":\"Node centrality measures are a poor substitute for causal inference - PMC\",\"url\":\"https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6497646/\"},{\"title\":\"Degree Centrality - an overview | ScienceDirect Topics\",\"url\":\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/computer-science/degree-centrality\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's comprehensive information about the topics in your query:\n\n## Betweenness Centrality\n\nBetweenness centrality is a measure of centrality in a graph based on shortest paths, measuring how frequently a node appears on the shortest path between other nodes in the graph. It is a way of detecting the amount of influence a node has over the flow of information in a graph.\n\n## Weighted Networks\n\nIn a weighted network the links connecting the nodes are no longer treated as binary interactions, but are weighted in proportion to their capacity, influence, frequency, etc., which adds another dimension of heterogeneity within the network beyond the topological effects.\n\nIn a weighted social network, an individual's betweenness centrality takes into account not only how often they bridge different groups but also the weighted significance of the interactions they facilitate. If the weights represent the strength of friendships, for instance, a person with high betweenness centrality might not only connect groups but also facilitate more meaningful or influential interactions.\n\n## In-Degree and Node Importance\n\nMany centrality measures effectively count the number of paths (also called walks) of some type going through a given vertex. Betweenness centrality focuses not just on overall connectedness but occupying positions that are pivotal to the network's connectivity.\n\n## DAG and Convergence\n\nUsing the causal framework based on directed acyclic graphs (DAGs), the relation between causal influence and node centrality measures is not straightforward. The left-skew and negative correlation is less pronounced for betweenness as it takes into account global information. Eigenvector centrality does predict the node with the highest causal influence. By transforming the DAG into a network, eigenvector centrality considers the transitive importance of nodes, which allows increased weight further down the original DAG structure to be propagated back to causally important variables.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"03703c3c-9401-4dfa-a3fc-94c2957370c9","timestamp":"2026-03-30T04:39:46.652Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9cb3c6ef-f98a-4103-a6d5-cbd31cfda8d4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/root/tradingDesk","sessionId":"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4","version":"2.1.87","gitBranch":"main","slug":"vectorized-kindling-hoare"}

Intelligence Infrastructure\",\"url\":\"https://nixus.pro/\"},{\"title\":\"Using OSINT in geopolitical assessment: a practical guide\",\"url\":\"https://www.authentic8.com/blog/osint-geopolitical-assessment\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about these platforms:\n\n## NEXUS Intelligence\n\nNEXUS integrates geopolitical, market, OSINT, and systemic risk signals into one platform with AI-driven convergence analysis, game theory scenarios, and prediction tracking for analysts, traders, and institutions. \n\nThe platform has four primary layers: Geopolitical (conflicts, sanctions, diplomacy), Market (options flow, volatility, credit spreads), OSINT (flight tracking, shipping, social media), and Systemic Risk (regime detection, macro indicators).\n\nEvery prediction is tracked with Brier scores and published transparently, with scoring that separates directional calls from level estimates and filters stale predictions to maintain statistical rigour.\n\n## Market Ontology\n\nMarket Ontology appears to be a related platform focused on causal transmission analysis. It is a macro, policy, and geopolitical intelligence platform that tracks how shocks transmit through markets, combining data from 40+ sources into daily regime detection, risk mapping, causal transmission analysis, and cross-asset monitoring.\n\n**Regarding Open Source:** The search results discuss these as commercial intelligence platforms rather than open-source projects. CME Group\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.cmegroup.com/markets/agriculture/fertilizer/urea-granular-fob-middle-east.html\\\"},{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Developing Situation: Futures Spike, Retail Catch-Up, and Farmer Fallout from Middle East Fertilizer Supply Squeeze\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://ncga.com/stay-informed/media/the-corn-economy/article/2026/03/developing-situation-futures-spike-retail-catch-up-and-farmer-fallout-from-middle-east-fertilizer-supply-squeeze\\\"}]\\n\\nBased on the search results, here's what's happening with CME urea futures prices in March 2026 amid the Hormuz closure:\\n\\n## Price Surge Overview\\n\\nThe nearby Gulf Free-On-Board (FOB) March 2026 urea contract jumped $128 per ton between February 27 and March 3 following the geopolitical crisis. Urea prices are up more than 28% within three weeks of the disruption.\\n\\nMore dramatically, urea hit $683 per metric ton at the New Orleans import hub on March 5, up from $516 the week before—representing a 32% spike in a single week.\\n\\n## The Hormuz Disruption Catalyst\\n\\nThe Strait of Hormuz, a critical shipping route that runs along Iran's southern border, has been severely disrupted since the start of the war, with traffic effectively coming to a halt and several ships being hit by projectiles. Around one-third of the global seaborne fertilizer trade passes through the Strait of Hormuz, making this closure particularly impactful for urea markets.\\n\\n## Market Impact\\n\\nThe cost of FOB granular urea in Egypt jumped to around $700 per metric ton, up from $400 to $490 before the war began. The average price for urea (46-0-0) rose to $822.50 per ton, an increase of $231.50 (+39%) from the prior report, according to USDA data.\\n\\n\\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.\"}]},\"uuid\":\"a6088d2f-6bfc-43ad-950f-e3f1c8de29ea\",\"timestamp\":\"2026-03-30T02:24:26.847Z\",\"sourceToolAssistantUUID\":\"6404b339-3f16-48b3-b36f-805de901b7ef\",\"userType\":\"external\",\"entrypoint\":\"cli\",\"cwd\":\"/root/tradingDesk\",\"sessionId\":\"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4\",\"version\":\"2.1.87\",\"gitBranch\":\"main\",\"slug\":\"vectorized-kindling-hoare\"}\n2\t{\"parentUuid\":\"211c14ea-d321-4cab-8587-53ede921bb54\",\"isSidechain\":true,\"promptId\":\"3e9946ec-7703-4193-9075-c03c2a7b1e94\",\"agentId\":\"ac847309132dd78dd\",\"type\":\"user\",\"message\":{\"role\":\"user\",\"content\":[{\"tool_use_id\":\"toolu_014sNqbQ9GJpRNpNX93tW2RJ\",\"type\":\"tool_result\",\"content\":\"Web search results for query: \\\"spring planting window 2026 fertilizer shortage crop impact timeline corn wheat\\\"\\n\\nLinks: [{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Fertilizer shortages threaten spring planting in US, Canada | The Pig Site\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.thepigsite.com/news/2026/03/fertilizer-shortages-threaten-spring-planting-in-us-canada\\\"},{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Fertilizer Supply Problems Put Farmers in a Tough Spot | Focus on Ag | American Farm Bureau Federation\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.fb.org/focus-on-agriculture/fertilizer-supply-problems-put-farmers-in-a-tough-spot\\\"},{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Nations Race to Secure Enough Fertilizer and Prevent Food Crisis - AgWeb\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.agweb.com/news/fertilizer-prices-top-list-2026-profitability-threats-global-supply-tightens\\\"},{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Iran war brings more economic pain for American farmers\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.ms.now/news/iran-war-american-farmers-fertilizer\\\"},{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Fertilizer prices surge amid Iran war, sparking food security warnings\\\",\\\"url\\\":\\\"https://www.cnbc.com/2026/03/25/fertilizer-price-iran-war-food-security-inflation-urea-potash-nitrogen-farmers.html\\\"}]\\n\\nBased on the web search results, here's an overview of the spring planting window 2026 fertilizer shortage and its impact on crop production:\\n\\n## Fertilizer Shortage Overview\\n\\nFarmers in the US and Canada are now facing spring planting disruptions as they struggle to find fertilizer, with prices spiking more than a third since the war in Iran paralyzed global trade. The US is about 25% short of the usual supplies that farmers buy for spring planting.\\n\\n## Root Cause\\n\\nWith the Strait of Hormuz effectively shut, shipments have ground to a halt as Iran, the US and Israel continue to exchange strikes on energy infrastructure. More than one-third of globally traded fertilizer passes through the Strait of Hormuz.\\n\\n## Impact on Corn and Wheat\\n\\nFertilizer shortages could reduce yields for crops like corn, soybeans, wheat and rice, as the timing is critical because fertilizers are applied early in the crop cycle and help determine yields later in the year. Roughly 93 million acres of corn are expected to be planted in 2026.\\n\\n## Broader Implications\\n\\nWhat's unfolding is not a temporary spike but a structural disruption with consequences that could echo through American agriculture for years. Unless critical farm inputs are strategically prioritized, the U.S. risks a shortfall in crops, which is a threat to food security and national security, and could contribute to inflationary pressures.\\n\\n\\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.\"}]},\"uuid\":\"cfb5dfcf-193c-4d3f-b779-28d0f288b200\",\"timestamp\":\"2026-03-30T02:24:27.078Z\",\"sourceToolAssistantUUID\":\"211c14ea-d321-4cab-8587-53ede921bb54\",\"userType\":\"external\",\"entrypoint\":\"cli\",\"cwd\":\"/root/tradingDesk\",\"sessionId\":\"0f6dc30f-3205-48b9-9e89-f30683400ad4\",\"version\":\"2.1.87\",\"gitBranch\":\"main\",\"slug\":\"vectorized-kindling-hoare\"}\n3\t{\"parentUuid\":\"f353439c-8e10-4c39-8ed5-67bce8eb4d6e\",\"isSidechain\":true,\"promptId\":\"3e9946ec-7703-4193-9075-c03c2a7b1e94\",\"agentId\":\"ac847309132dd78dd\",\"type\":\"user\",\"message\":{\"role\":\"user\",\"content\":[{\"tool_use_id\":\"toolu_017Rnda7BTM8oJJDjBBvrXfS\",\"type\":\"tool_result\",\"content\":\"Web search results for query: \\\"Iran Hormuz selective transit China yuan oil settlement March 2026\\\"\\n\\nLinks: [{\\\"title\\\":\\\"Iran's Hormuz yuan play a direct hit on the petrodollar - Here are the key findings:\\n\\n## Iran's Strait of Hormuz Yuan Payment Proposal\\n\\nIran announced a diplomatic and economic initiative in mid-March 2026, under which Tehran proposed permitting limited oil tanker passage through the Strait of Hormuz on the condition that associated oil cargo payments be denominated in Chinese yuan, amid escalating US-Iran tensions and partial maritime disruptions.\\n\\n## Implementation Details\\n\\nIran has established a de facto toll system at the Strait of Hormuz, allowing select vessels passage in exchange for fees paid in Chinese yuan, with China and India receiving preferential access while Western carriers remain blocked.\\n\\nA fee — estimated at $0.50 to $1.20 per barrel of cargo — is negotiated and paid in Chinese yuan via accounts held at Kunlun Bank, the Chinese bank that has historically processed Iran-China transactions outside the SWIFT system.\\n\\n## Strategic Context\\n\\nIf Iran is allowing limited tanker passage only if transactions are settled in Chinese yuan, then the issue at stake is no longer merely the movement of ships, but the future architecture of global energy finance.\\n\\nAccording to Lloyd's, at least two vessels that have transited the strait so far have paid a fee in yuan, with one transit brokered by a Chinese maritime services company acting as an intermediary, which also handled the payment to Iranian authorities.\\n\\nThis represents a significant geopolitical and economic development, with implications for the global petrodollar system.\\n\\n\\nREMINDER: You MUST include the However, debt affordability is deteriorating across sovereigns, with emerging markets shifting toward non-USD issuance that reduces FX risk but often increases total borrowing costs.\\n\\n## Key Risks Identified\\n\\nSeveral critical vulnerabilities exist: Debt/GDP ratios will remain high, limiting the ability to absorb future shocks, particularly for emerging economies. Additionally, emerging markets tend to be more exposed to extreme weather events than advanced economies but have fewer resources for adaptation, with nearly half of EM sovereigns having high or very high credit exposure to physical climate risks but relatively weak fiscal strength.\\n\\n## Vulnerable Countries\\n\\nEl Salvador, Tunisia, Turkiye and Pakistan stand out as highly exposed to tighter global funding conditions, while sovereigns that have already defaulted on external debt like Sri Lanka and Zambia, or countries in the Caa3 or lower category including Suriname, Argentina and Ukraine are in the highest risk group.\\n\\n## Impact of Recent Downgrades\\n\\nRegarding rating actions, Moody's downgraded the US from Aaa to Aa1 on May 16, 2025. Let me now compile the comprehensive research report.\n\n---\n\nHere is the complete research report on building a causal reasoning graph / DAG system for the tradingDesk project.\n\n---\n\n## 1. DAG / Graph Libraries\n\n### Python: NetworkX (Recommended for Backend)\n\nNetworkX 3.6.1 is the standard Python graph library. It fully supports what you need:\n\n- **Weighted directed graphs**: `G = nx.DiGraph()` with `G.add_edge('Hormuz Closure', 'Oil Supply Loss', weight=0.95, lag_months=0, transmission='direct')`\n- **Arbitrary node/edge attributes**: Every node and edge holds a key/value attribute dictionary. You can store `threshold`, `current_value`, `status`, `confidence`, `lag_months`, etc.\n- **DAG algorithms**: `nx.is_directed_acyclic_graph(G)`, `nx.topological_sort(G)`, `nx.ancestors(G, node)`, `nx.descendants(G, node)`, `nx.all_simple_paths(G, source, target)`\n- **Centrality for convergence detection**: `nx.in_degree_centrality(G)`, `nx.betweenness_centrality(G)`, `nx.eigenvector_centrality(G)`\n- **Zero external dependencies** beyond Python stdlib + numpy (optional)\n- **Export to JSON** for feeding into JS frontends: `nx.node_link_data(G)` produces a dict with `nodes` and `links` arrays that maps directly to Cytoscape.js or vis.js data formats\n\n**Key pattern for your propagation engine**: Topological sort the DAG, then iterate in order, computing each node's value based on its incoming edges. This is the core algorithm you need -- not Bayesian inference, not causal discovery. Just: \"walk the graph in dependency order, apply edge weights and lag offsets.\"\n\n```python\nfor node in nx.topological_sort(G):\n    for pred in G.predecessors(node):\n        edge = G.edges[pred, node]\n        impact = nodes[pred]['shock'] * edge['weight'] * edge['attenuation']\n        if edge['lag_months'] <= current_horizon:\n            nodes[node]['accumulated_impact'] += impact\n```\n\n### JavaScript: Cytoscape.js + cytoscape-dagre (Recommended for Frontend)\n\nAfter measuring actual bundle sizes from CDN:\n\n| Library | Minified | Gzipped | CDN URL |\n|---------|----------|---------|---------|\n| **Cytoscape.js** | **365 KB** | **114 KB** | `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/cytoscape@3.30.4/dist/cytoscape.min.js` |\n| cytoscape-dagre (layout plugin) | 12 KB | 4 KB | `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/cytoscape-dagre@2.5.0/cytoscape-dagre.js` |\n| dagre (standalone, layout only) | 277 KB | 64 KB | `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/dagre@0.8.5/dist/dagre.min.js` |\n| vis-network (standalone) | **686 KB** | **163 KB** | `https://unpkg.com/vis-network@9.1.6/standalone/umd/vis-network.min.js` |\n| Mermaid.js (full) | ~2.7 MB | ~800 KB | Too large for inline |\n| Mermaid Tiny | ~1.6 MB | ~500 KB | Still too large |\n| d3-dag | ~45 KB | ~15 KB | `https://unpkg.com/d3-dag@1.1.0` (ESM only, requires D3 core) |\n\n**Recommendation: Cytoscape.js + cytoscape-dagre.** Here's why:\n\n1. **Total inline cost**: 377 KB minified (cytoscape + dagre plugin), which is comparable to your existing commodity book HTML (57 KB content + the fonts you already load). Gzipped total ~118 KB.\n2. **Dagre layout is built for DAGs**: Hierarchical ranking algorithm (Sugiyama-style) that produces clean top-to-bottom or left-to-right DAG layouts. Configurable `rankDir`, `rankSep`, `nodeSep`.\n3. **State-based styling via classes**: You can define CSS-like selectors (`node.fired { background-color: red }`, `node.approaching { background-color: orange }`) and toggle with `node.addClass('fired')`. This maps directly to your trigger states.\n4. **Event handling**: `cy.on('tap', 'node', fn)` for click, `cy.on('mouseover', 'node', fn)` for hover. Tooltips via native DOM positioning from `node.renderedPosition()` -- no need for popper/tippy dependencies.\n5. **Programmatic data updates**: `node.data('impact', 0.85)` triggers re-render. Style can map data to visuals: `'background-color': 'mapData(impact, 0, 1, #333, #ff4444)'`.\n6. **Can be fully inlined in a single HTML file** -- UMD build, no module system required. This matches the bookgen.py pattern exactly.\n\n**Why NOT vis.js**: 686 KB minified (nearly 2x cytoscape), slower rendering for complex graphs, and the physics simulation it's famous for is actually wrong for DAGs (you want fixed hierarchical layout, not force-directed bouncing).\n\n**Why NOT Mermaid.js**: 2.7 MB is unacceptable for an inline HTML file. Also: Mermaid renders to SVG via a text DSL, which means limited interactivity (click events require `securityLevel: 'loose'`), no programmatic node updates, and no way to change node colors at runtime. It's a documentation tool, not a visualization runtime.\n\n**Why NOT D3 raw**: D3 gives pixel-level control but requires building everything from scratch. For a DAG with tooltips, state coloring, and click events, you'd write 500+ lines of D3 code to get what Cytoscape gives you in 50 lines.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Causal Inference / Bayesian Network Libraries: Assessment\n\n### pgmpy\n- Full Bayesian network toolkit (structure learning, parameter estimation, inference)\n- Supports Dynamic Bayesian Networks (DBNs) with 2-Time-Slice representation -- relevant for modeling temporal lag\n- **Verdict: Overkill.** You don't need conditional probability tables or belief propagation. Your edges have known weights from domain expertise, not learned from data. pgmpy would force you to discretize continuous variables and define CPDs, which adds complexity without value.\n\n### CausalNex (McKinsey/QuantumBlack)\n- Built on NetworkX's DiGraph (it subclasses it as `StructureModel`)\n- Supports what-if analysis: \"what if this variable was different, what's the downstream impact?\"\n- Uses Do-calculus for interventions\n- **Verdict: Interesting but wrong abstraction.** CausalNex wants to learn structure from data and then do interventions. You already know your graph structure (it comes from your thesis). And your \"what-if\" is simpler: \"if Brent hits $130, propagate the shock.\" You don't need Pearl's do-calculus for that.\n\n### DoWhy (Microsoft)\n- Causal inference library focused on identifying, estimating, and refuting causal effects\n- Requires observational data to estimate effects\n- **Verdict: Not relevant.** DoWhy answers \"does X cause Y in this dataset?\" You already know X causes Y -- you want to model the magnitude and timing.\n\n### What you actually need\n\nNone of these. Your propagation model is simpler than any of them assume:\n\n```\nFor each node in topological order:\n    node.shock = sum(\n        predecessor.shock * edge.weight * decay(edge.lag, horizon)\n        for predecessor, edge in incoming_edges\n    )\n```\n\nThis is a weighted topological propagation, not Bayesian inference. NetworkX's `topological_sort()` + your own 20-line propagation function gives you everything. The weights come from your domain judgment (e.g., \"Hormuz closure transmits 0.9x to oil supply loss\" or \"fertilizer shortage transmits 0.7x to planting miss with 2-month lag\").\n\n---\n\n## 3. Scenario / What-If Analysis on a DAG\n\n### The Pattern: Shock Propagation via Topological Walk\n\nThe standard approach for scenario analysis on a DAG is:\n\n1. **Define the shock**: Set one or more root nodes to a shock value (e.g., `hormuz_closure.shock = 1.0`)\n2. **Topological propagation**: Walk the graph in dependency order. Each node accumulates impact from predecessors via edge weights.\n3. **Horizon filtering**: Each edge has a `lag_months` attribute. When running a \"3-month scenario,\" only edges with `lag <= 3` contribute.\n4. **Attenuation**: Impact decays through the chain. Each edge's `weight` (0.0-1.0) acts as a transmission coefficient.\n5. **Convergence amplification**: When multiple paths converge on a node, the impacts sum (or use max, or a custom function). A node receiving signal from 3 independent paths is a stronger signal than one receiving from 1.\n\n### Monte Carlo Extension\n\nFor uncertainty, you don't need full probabilistic programming. A simple Monte Carlo wrapper:\n\n1. Each edge weight has a range (e.g., `weight: 0.7, weight_std: 0.15`)\n2. Run the topological propagation 1000 times, sampling edge weights from their distributions\n3. The output for each terminal node is a distribution of impacts\n4. Report P10/P50/P90 confidence bands\n\nThis is 30 lines of Python on top of the base propagation function. No PyMC, no Stan, no MCMC chains.\n\n### Influence Diagram Pattern\n\nThe commercial tools (DPL, Analytica) model decision problems with three node types: decisions, uncertainties, and values. Your thesis graph maps to this: trigger nodes are uncertainties, position sizing is decisions, P&L outcomes are values. But you don't need their software -- the DAG propagation above covers the analytical need, and Cytoscape.js covers the visual.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Transmission Lag Modeling\n\n### How Macro Economists Model It\n\nThe standard academic approach is **Vector Autoregression (VAR) with Impulse Response Functions (IRFs)**:\n\n- A VAR model captures lagged relationships between variables: each variable is regressed on its own lags and the lags of all other variables\n- An IRF traces the effect of a one-unit shock to one variable on all other variables over time\n- The response builds, peaks, and decays -- capturing both the lag and the persistence of the shock\n\n### Key Empirical Lags from the Literature\n\nFrom the IMF and Federal Reserve research on oil shock transmission:\n\n| Transmission Path | Lag (from shock) | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Oil price --> gasoline/diesel price | 0-2 weeks | Direct pass-through |\n| Oil price --> transportation cost | 1-3 months | Contract repricing |\n| Oil price --> fertilizer price | 1-2 months | Input cost pass-through |\n| Oil price --> wage pressure (AE) | ~3 quarters (9 months) | IMF WP 2025 |\n| Oil price --> wage pressure (EM) | ~6 quarters (18 months) | IMF WP 2025 |\n| Oil price --> employment (aggregate) | 3-6 months | Hamilton, Herrera et al. |\n| Fertilizer shortage --> planting miss | Calendar-driven (Apr 15 deadline) | agronomic constraint |\n| Planting miss --> food price spike | 4-6 months (harvest cycle) | Growing season |\n| Food spike --> EM sovereign stress | 1-3 months | Import bill pressure |\n\n### How to Model It in Your DAG\n\nEach edge gets a `lag_months` attribute:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"from\": \"Hormuz Closure\",\n  \"to\": \"Oil Supply Loss\",\n  \"weight\": 0.95,\n  \"lag_months\": 0,\n  \"note\": \"Immediate -- shipping stops within days\"\n}\n```\n\n```json\n{\n  \"from\": \"Brent Price Spike\",\n  \"to\": \"Employment Contraction\",\n  \"weight\": 0.4,\n  \"lag_months\": 4,\n  \"note\": \"Hamilton 2003: oil shocks take 3-6 months to hit employment\"\n}\n```\n\nThe propagation engine filters by horizon:\n\n```python\ndef propagate(G, horizon_months):\n    for node in nx.topological_sort(G):\n        for pred in G.predecessors(node):\n            edge = G.edges[pred, node]\n            if edge['lag_months'] <= horizon_months:\n                time_decay = 1.0 - (edge['lag_months'] / (horizon_months + 1)) * 0.3\n                G.nodes[node]['impact'] += G.nodes[pred]['impact'] * edge['weight'] * time_decay\n```\n\nThis lets you run \"what does the world look like in 1 month?\" vs \"3 months?\" vs \"12 months?\" and see the shock wavefront move through the graph.\n\n### Time-Lagged Causal Graphs (Academic Reference)\n\nThe formal framework is called a \"window causal graph\" or \"full-time causal graph\" where nodes are `(variable, time_slice)` pairs. Tigramite (Python library, `github.com/jakobrunge/tigramite`) implements this for causal discovery from time series data. For your use case, you don't need causal discovery -- you're building the graph from domain knowledge -- but the concept of edges carrying lag information is the same.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Signal Confluence / Convergence Detection\n\n### The Core Insight\n\nWhen multiple independent causal paths converge on the same node, that node is a \"convergence point\" or \"fan-in node.\" In your Iran/Hormuz thesis:\n\n```\nHormuz Closure --> Oil Supply Loss --> Brent Price --> Diesel --> Freight Cost --|\n                                                                                 |--> Employment Contraction\nHormuz Closure --> Fertilizer Shortage --> Planting Miss --> Food Spike ---------|\n```\n\nEmployment Contraction receives signal from TWO independent transmission chains. That's a stronger signal than if only one path existed.\n\n### Scoring Convergence Strength\n\nThree complementary metrics, all available in NetworkX:\n\n1. **Weighted in-degree**: `sum(edge['weight'] for edge in incoming_edges)`. Simple, effective. A node with 4 incoming edges at weight 0.6 each has convergence strength 2.4.\n\n2. **Number of independent root ancestors**: How many distinct root-level shocks feed into this node? Use `nx.ancestors(G, node)` intersected with root nodes (in-degree 0). If a node is reachable from 3 different root shocks, it has higher confluence than one reachable from only 1.\n\n3. **Betweenness centrality** on the DAG: Measures how many shortest paths pass through a node. High betweenness = critical bottleneck or convergence point. `nx.betweenness_centrality(G, weight='weight')`.\n\n4. **Path-count scoring**: Count `len(list(nx.all_simple_paths(G, root, node)))` for each root. More paths = more ways the shock can reach this node = higher cumulative exposure.\n\n### Practical Formula for Your System\n\n```python\ndef convergence_score(G, node):\n    roots = [n for n in G.nodes() if G.in_degree(n) == 0]\n    path_count = sum(len(list(nx.all_simple_paths(G, r, node))) for r in roots if nx.has_path(G, r, node))\n    independent_sources = sum(1 for r in roots if nx.has_path(G, r, node))\n    weighted_indegree = sum(G.edges[pred, node]['weight'] for pred in G.predecessors(node))\n    return {\n        'path_count': path_count,\n        'independent_sources': independent_sources,\n        'weighted_indegree': weighted_indegree,\n        'composite': path_count * 0.3 + independent_sources * 0.5 + weighted_indegree * 0.2\n    }\n```\n\nNodes with high composite scores are your \"strong confluence\" points -- where the thesis is most validated if multiple chains fire simultaneously.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Rendering in a Single HTML File\n\n### Recommended Stack: Cytoscape.js + cytoscape-dagre, Inlined\n\nTotal size for the JS libraries when inlined:\n\n- `cytoscape.min.js`: 365 KB\n- `cytoscape-dagre.js`: 12 KB (this bundles dagre internally)\n- **Total: ~377 KB of JS to inline**\n\nFor comparison, your existing `iran-hormuz.html` is 57 KB. Adding the graph viewer would bring total HTML to ~450 KB. Still well within browser limits, still opens instantly.\n\n### Implementation Pattern\n\n```html\n<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html>\n<head>\n  <style>\n    #graph { width: 100%; height: 600px; }\n    .tooltip { position: absolute; background: #1a1a2e; ... }\n  </style>\n  <script>/* cytoscape.min.js inlined here */</script>\n  <script>/* cytoscape-dagre.js inlined here */</script>\n</head>\n<body>\n  <div id=\"graph\"></div>\n  <script>\n    const data = /* JSON generated by Python */;\n    const cy = cytoscape({\n      container: document.getElementById('graph'),\n      elements: data.elements,\n      style: [\n        { selector: 'node', style: { 'background-color': 'data(color)', 'label': 'data(label)' } },\n        { selector: 'node.fired', style: { 'background-color': '#ff4444', 'border-width': 3 } },\n        { selector: 'node.approaching', style: { 'background-color': '#e69a4c' } },\n        { selector: 'edge', style: { 'width': 'data(weight)', 'line-color': '#666', 'target-arrow-shape': 'triangle' } }\n      ],\n      layout: { name: 'dagre', rankDir: 'LR', nodeSep: 50, rankSep: 100 }\n    });\n    \n    // Tooltip on hover\n    cy.on('mouseover', 'node', function(e) {\n      const node = e.target;\n      showTooltip(node.renderedPosition(), node.data());\n    });\n  </script>\n</body>\n</html>\n```\n\nThe Python generator (`thesisgraph.py` or similar) would:\n1. Read the thesis graph JSON config\n2. Run propagation with NetworkX\n3. Compute convergence scores\n4. Serialize to Cytoscape.js elements format\n5. Inline the JS libraries + data into the HTML template\n6. Write the single-file HTML\n\nThis matches the bookgen.py pattern exactly: **JSON config --> Python generator --> single-file HTML**.\n\n### Alternative: vis-network\n\nIf you want physics-based \"breathing\" animation (nodes that settle into position), vis-network works. But at 686 KB it's nearly 2x the size, and its hierarchical layout is weaker than dagre. Not recommended for DAGs specifically.\n\n### Alternative: Pure SVG with dagre\n\nIf you want absolute minimum size, you could use dagre standalone (277 KB) to compute layout, then render with raw SVG. No canvas library needed. But you lose click/hover/zoom interactions that Cytoscape provides for free.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Existing Financial / Geopolitical Causal Graph Tools\n\n### Commercial Platforms\n\n**Market Ontology** (`marketontology.com`): The closest thing to what you're building, but as a SaaS product. Tracks how macro/policy/geopolitical shocks transmit through markets. 40+ data sources, daily regime detection, causal transmission analysis, cross-asset monitoring. Commercial, not open-source.\n\n**NEXUS Intelligence** (`nexushq.xyz`): Four-layer intelligence platform (Geopolitical, Market, OSINT, Systemic Risk) with AI-driven convergence analysis and game theory scenarios. Tracks predictions with Brier scores. Commercial.\n\n**Permutable AI** (`permutable.ai`): Extracts cause-and-effect relationships between political events and asset movements. Models and anticipates market impact of global developments. Commercial.\n\n**Palantir Gotham**: The reference implementation for graph-based intelligence analysis. Uses ontology models, link charts, graph algorithms, and geospatial mapping. Strictly government/enterprise, multi-million-dollar contracts.\n\n### Academic\n\n**\"Computational Global Macro: Game Theory, Machine Learning, and Causal Inference\"** by Joseph Simonian (World Scientific, Jan 2025, 188 pages). The only book that explicitly combines causal inference with macro/geopolitical analysis for investment. Covers game theory for modeling state interactions, ML for regime detection, and causal inference for intervention analysis. This is the closest academic reference to what tradingDesk is building.\n\n### Open Source\n\n**There is nothing open-source that does what you're describing.** The closest tools are:\n\n- **DAGitty** (`dagitty.net`): Browser-based causal diagram editor for epidemiology. Good for drawing DAGs but no propagation, no financial data, no scenario analysis.\n- **CausalNex** (McKinsey): Open-source Python library built on NetworkX for Bayesian causal graphs. Supports what-if analysis but designed for data-learned structures, not hand-built thesis graphs.\n- **Tigramite**: Causal discovery from time series. Academic tool, not for manual graph construction.\n\n**This means tradingDesk would be novel** -- an open-source thesis-driven causal reasoning system for macro trading. The combination of hand-built causal DAG + weighted propagation + lag modeling + convergence scoring + interactive visualization in a single HTML file does not exist as an open tool.\n\n---\n\n## Summary: Recommended Architecture\n\n### Backend (Python, zero new dependencies beyond NetworkX)\n\n| Component | Library | Notes |\n|-----------|---------|-------|\n| Graph structure | `networkx.DiGraph` | Weighted edges, node/edge attributes |\n| Topological propagation | `nx.topological_sort()` + custom 20-line function | Walk DAG in order, apply weights/lags |\n| Convergence scoring | `nx.ancestors()`, `nx.all_simple_paths()`, in-degree | Composite metric |\n| Monte Carlo | `random.gauss()` in a loop | Sample edge weights, run propagation N times |\n| JSON export | `nx.node_link_data()` or custom serializer | Feed to Cytoscape.js format |\n| HTML generation | String templating (same pattern as bookgen.py) | Inline JS + data into template |\n\n### Frontend (JS, inlined in HTML)\n\n| Component | Library | Size (minified) | CDN |\n|-----------|---------|-----------------|-----|\n| Graph rendering | Cytoscape.js | 365 KB | `cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/cytoscape@3.30.4/dist/cytoscape.min.js` |\n| DAG layout | cytoscape-dagre | 12 KB | `cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/cytoscape-dagre@2.5.0/cytoscape-dagre.js` |\n| Tooltips | Native DOM (no library needed) | 0 KB | -- |\n| **Total** | | **377 KB** | |\n\n### Graph JSON Config Pattern\n\n```json\n{\n  \"title\": \"Iran/Hormuz Thesis Graph\",\n  \"nodes\": [\n    { \"id\": \"hormuz_closure\", \"label\": \"Hormuz Closure\", \"type\": \"root_shock\", \"current_probability\": 0.85 },\n    { \"id\": \"oil_supply_loss\", \"label\": \"Oil Supply Loss (~10 mbpd)\", \"type\": \"transmission\", \"threshold\": 5.0, \"current\": 10.2 },\n    { \"id\": \"brent_price\", \"label\": \"Brent >$130\", \"type\": \"trigger\", \"threshold\": 130, \"current\": 127.5 },\n    { \"id\": \"fertilizer_shortage\", \"label\": \"Fertilizer Shortage\", \"type\": \"transmission\" },\n    { \"id\": \"planting_miss\", \"label\": \"Corn Belt Planting Miss\", \"type\": \"calendar_event\", \"deadline\": \"2026-04-15\" },\n    { \"id\": \"employment_contraction\", \"label\": \"Employment Contraction\", \"type\": \"terminal\", \"convergence_score\": null },\n    { \"id\": \"em_sovereign_stress\", \"label\": \"EM Sovereign Stress\", \"type\": \"terminal\", \"convergence_score\": null }\n  ],\n  \"edges\": [\n    { \"from\": \"hormuz_closure\", \"to\": \"oil_supply_loss\", \"weight\": 0.95, \"lag_months\": 0 },\n    { \"from\": \"oil_supply_loss\", \"to\": \"brent_price\", \"weight\": 0.85, \"lag_months\": 0 },\n    { \"from\": \"brent_price\", \"to\": \"diesel_cost\", \"weight\": 0.7, \"lag_months\": 1 },\n    { \"from\": \"diesel_cost\", \"to\": \"freight_cost\", \"weight\": 0.8, \"lag_months\": 1 },\n    { \"from\": \"freight_cost\",
